# Skin 2: Expericapades — Experimental Physics

## 1. Domain Overview & Core Fault Lines

Experimental physics is the art and discipline of interrogating nature through engineered apparatus, controlled noise, and calibrated inference. Far from being a passive verifier of theory, experimental physics has its own autonomous traditions and five enduring fault lines:

1. **Big Science Megaprojects vs. Tabletop Precision Physics:** Should experimental physics concentrate resources in flagship multi-billion-dollar colliders, interferometers, and space missions, or invest heavily in nimble, PI-led tabletop experiments pushing quantum precision? (Weinberg 1961; Galison & Hevly 1992; DeMille et al. 2017).
2. **Theory-Testing vs. Autonomous Exploratory Experimentation:** Does experiment exist chiefly to adjudicate theoretical hypotheses, or does building new instruments and probing unexplored parameter regimes drive discovery independently of theory? (Popper 1959 vs. Hacking 1983; Steinle 1997).
3. **Galison's "Image" vs. "Logic" Traditions:** Should experimental evidence aspire to unmistakable, visually inspectable single "golden events" (tracks, spectra, interferograms), or embrace high-rate electronic triggers, statistical background subtraction, and large-scale Monte Carlo pipelines? (Galison 1997).
4. **Ironclad Conservatism ($5\sigma$ & Blinding) vs. Early Anomaly Sharing:** Should collaborations enforce strict blind analyses, independent duplicate pipelines, and $5\sigma$ thresholds before claiming anything, or publish intriguing $2\sigma\text{–}3\sigma$ tensions early to mobilize independent checks? (Klein & Roodman 2005; Lyons 2013).
5. **Home-Built "String and Sealing Wax" Craft vs. Standardized Commercial & Facility Infrastructure:** Should experimentalists machine, solder, and calibrate every subsystem in-house to master every systematic effect, or assemble standardized commercial hardware and shared national facilities to maximize physics throughput?

## 2. Categories

1. **Epistemology of Experiment & Discovery** — Autonomy from theory, image vs. logic, closure of experiments, replication, and null results.
2. **Measurement, Metrology & Precision** — Energy vs. precision frontiers, systematic budgets, absolute vs. differential methods, and quantum-enhanced sensing.
3. **Instrumentation, Detectors & Hardware Craft** — Home-built vs. turnkey hardware, analog vs. digital front-ends, detector priorities, and extreme environments.
4. **Data Analysis, Blinding & Statistical Standards** — $5\sigma$ thresholds, blind analysis, Frequentist vs. Bayesian inference, Monte Carlo vs. data-driven backgrounds, and ML reconstruction.
5. **Laboratory Practice & Experimental Craft** — Debugging ethos, manual vs. automated alignment, documentation, sample ownership, and noise hygiene.
6. **Collaboration, Scale & Experimental Culture** — Big vs. small science, mega-collaboration authorship, open data, risk management, and generalist vs. specialist roles.
7. **Astroparticle, Observational & Multi-Messenger Frontiers** — Space vs. ground observatories, gravitational-wave & neutrino messengers, synoptic surveys, balloon missions, and dark-matter complementarity.
8. **Quantum Control, AMO & Ultrafast Laser Physics** — Cold-atom & trapped-ion platforms, CW precision vs. attosecond pulses, cavity QED, single-shot readout, and plasma wakefield accelerators.
9. **Accelerator, Beam & Condensed-Matter Probes** — Lepton vs. hadron colliders, circular vs. linear machines, neutron/X-ray/STM complementarity, transport vs. thermodynamic proof, and pulsed mega-fields.

## 3. Topics (52 Topics)

### Category 1: Epistemology of Experiment & Discovery

#### `EX-01` — Autonomy of Experiment from Theory
- **Category:** Epistemology of Experiment & Discovery
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Theory-Testing Servant` | `Iterative Co-Evolution` | `Autonomous Exploratory Science`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should experiments primarily be designed around sharp theoretical predictions, or should experimentalists explore uncharted physical regimes without waiting for a theorist's blessing?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Theory-Testing Servant:* Without a clear theoretical hypothesis and falsifiable prediction, an experiment degenerates into expensive stamp collecting.
  - *Iterative Co-Evolution:* Theory guides where to look, while unexpected experimental anomalies force theory to reinvent itself.
  - *Autonomous Exploratory Science:* Experiment has a life of its own; opening a new order of magnitude in sensitivity or resolution routinely uncovers physics no theorist predicted.
- **Primary citations:** K. Popper (1959), *The Logic of Scientific Discovery*; I. Hacking (1983), *Representing and Intervening*; F. Steinle (1997), "Entering New Fields: Exploratory Uses of Experimentation," *Philos. Sci.*

#### `EX-02` — Image Tradition vs. Logic Tradition
- **Category:** Epistemology of Experiment & Discovery
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Golden-Event Direct Image` | `Hybrid Event-Display & Cuts` | `High-Rate Statistical Logic`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Do you trust a handful of crystal-clear, individually reconstructed events (cloud/bubble chambers, direct imaging), or statistical excesses extracted from billions of electronic counts?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Golden-Event Direct Image:* One unmistakable, visually coherent event where every track and energy balance is clear beats a fragile statistical bump buried in background.
  - *Hybrid Event-Display & Cuts:* Combine high-rate automated filtering with human inspection of candidate events to catch unmodeled pathologies.
  - *High-Rate Statistical Logic:* Rare phenomena demand vast event rates, fast coincidence logic, and statistical likelihood fits that direct imaging can never achieve.
- **Primary citations:** P. Galison (1997), *Image and Logic: A Material Culture of Microphysics*.

#### `EX-03` — How Experiments End (Closure Criterion)
- **Category:** Epistemology of Experiment & Discovery
- **Geometry:** `triangle`
- **Poles:** `Independent Method Consilience` | `Exhaustive Systematic Closure` | `Pragmatic Community Consensus`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** When is an experimental result genuinely settled—when multiple distinct apparatus types agree, when all known systematic errors are bounded, or when the community stops finding productive objections?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Independent Method Consilience:* No single apparatus can guarantee it missed no systematic; true closure demands agreement across fundamentally different measurement techniques.
  - *Exhaustive Systematic Closure:* An experiment ends when its signal remains robust under every physically conceivable perturbation, calibration check, and null control.
  - *Pragmatic Community Consensus:* Absolute certainty never arrives; results close when further error reduction yields diminishing returns and other experiments build upon them.
- **Primary citations:** P. Galison (1987), *How Experiments End*; A. Franklin (1986), *The Neglect of Experiment*; H. M. Collins (1985), *Changing Order*.

#### `EX-04` — Uncharted Discovery vs. Independent Replication
- **Category:** Epistemology of Experiment & Discovery
- **Geometry:** `linear-sliders`
- **Poles:** `Chasing Uncharted Anomalies` | `Rigorous Independent Verification`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** How should experimental effort balance racing into unexplored phenomena versus dedicating premier apparatus and careers to reproducing and auditing others' headline claims?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Chasing Uncharted Anomalies:* Bold first measurements in virgin territory create paradigm shifts that incremental cross-checks never will.
  - *Rigorous Independent Verification:* Extraordinary claims—from room-temperature superconductors to Majorana zero modes—collapse without disciplined, well-funded independent replication.
- **Primary citations:** A. Franklin (1990), *Experiment, Right or Wrong*; H. M. Collins (1985), *Changing Order* ("experimenter's regress").

#### `EX-05` — Value of Null Results & Exclusion Limits
- **Category:** Epistemology of Experiment & Discovery
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Disappointing Dead End` | `Necessary Parameter Mapping` | `Triumph of Precision Craft`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** From Michelson–Morley to electron EDM and WIMP searches, experimental physics produces far more null results than detections; how should the field value them?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Disappointing Dead End:* Tightening an upper limit without finding a signal is a consolation prize compared to observing a real physical effect.
  - *Necessary Parameter Mapping:* Clean exclusion bounds steadily rule out whole classes of theories and steer the next generation of searches.
  - *Triumph of Precision Craft:* Achieving an order-of-magnitude tighter null bound often requires deeper instrumental mastery and carries more lasting theoretical impact than flashy tentative bumps.
- **Primary citations:** A. A. Michelson & E. W. Morley (1887), *Am. J. Sci.*; ACME Collaboration (2018), "Improved Limit on the Electric Dipole Moment of the Electron," *Nature*.

---

### Category 2: Measurement, Metrology & Precision

#### `EX-06` — Energy Frontier vs. Precision vs. Intensity Frontier
- **Category:** Measurement, Metrology & Precision
- **Geometry:** `triangle`
- **Poles:** `Highest-Energy Colliders` | `Ultra-Precision Tabletop & Clocks` | `High-Intensity Rare-Process Beams`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Where does the highest discovery-per-dollar leverage lie in probing fundamental physics—smashing particles at higher energy, measuring tiny shifts to 18 digits, or flooding detectors with intense beams?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Highest-Energy Colliders:* Only direct high-energy collisions can produce heavy new states on-shell and measure their couplings unambiguously.
  - *Ultra-Precision Tabletop & Clocks:* Optical clocks, atom interferometers, and EDM searches probe multi-TeV virtual physics on a laboratory bench at a fraction of collider cost.
  - *High-Intensity Rare-Process Beams:* Neutrino oscillations, flavor anomalies, and muon precision experiments reveal subtle symmetry violations hidden from energy-frontier machines.
- **Primary citations:** S. Weinberg (1993), *Dreams of a Final Theory*; D. DeMille, J. M. Doyle & A. O. Sushkov (2017), "Probing the Frontiers of Particle Physics with Tabletop-Scale Experiments," *Science*; Snowmass / European Strategy for Particle Physics reports.

#### `EX-07` — Statistical vs. Systematic Uncertainty Priority
- **Category:** Measurement, Metrology & Precision
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Maximize Raw Statistics First` | `Matched Stat-Syst Balance` | `Systematic-Control Obsession`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should an experiment first collect massive datasets and study systematics afterward, or refuse to integrate data until systematic drifts are engineered below statistical noise?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Maximize Raw Statistics First:* High signal-to-noise data lets you slice control regions finely and diagnose systematics you could never see in low-count runs.
  - *Matched Stat-Syst Balance:* Optimize the design so statistical and systematic uncertainties shrink together rather than letting either dominate blindly.
  - *Systematic-Control Obsession:* More statistics cannot save a biased apparatus; spend 80% of your effort characterizing and cancelling systematics before taking production data.
- **Primary citations:** R. Barlow (2002), "Systematic Errors: Facts and Fictions," *Durham Conf. Advanced Statistical Techniques in Particle Physics*; J. Heinrich & L. Lyons (2007), *Annu. Rev. Nucl. Part. Sci.*

#### `EX-08` — Absolute Metrology vs. Differential / Lock-In Measurement
- **Category:** Measurement, Metrology & Precision
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Absolute SI-Traceable Calibration` | `Dual Cross-Checked Strategy` | `Differential Null & Lock-In Mastery`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Is it better to measure an absolute physical quantity directly against primary standards, or modulate the signal and measure a differential ratio so common-mode drifts cancel?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Absolute SI-Traceable Calibration:* True physical comparability across labs and decades demands absolute, traceable calibration without hiding behind arbitrary relative units.
  - *Dual Cross-Checked Strategy:* Use modulation and nulling for sensitivity, then anchor the scale with periodic absolute calibration standards.
  - *Differential Null & Lock-In Mastery:* Nature whispers in $1/f$ noise; synchronous modulation, reversal symmetries, and null-balance bridges are the real secret to extreme precision.
- **Primary citations:** R. H. Dicke (1946), "The Measurement of Thermal Radiation at Microwave Frequencies," *Rev. Sci. Instrum.*; BIPM (2019), *The International System of Units (SI)* (9th ed.).

#### `EX-09` — Quantum-Enhanced Sensing Beyond the Standard Quantum Limit
- **Category:** Measurement, Metrology & Precision
- **Geometry:** `monopole-slider`
- **Poles:** `Commitment to Squeezed & Entangled Metrology`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** How aggressively should precision experiments deploy squeezed light, spin squeezing, and quantum non-demolition readout rather than squeezing every classical noise source first?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Commitment to Squeezed & Entangled Metrology:* Classical shot noise is no longer a hard wall; quantum-correlated states are now practical engineering tools that extend reach beyond classical power limits.
- **Primary citations:** C. M. Caves (1981), "Quantum-Mechanical Noise in an Interferometer," *Phys. Rev. D*; V. Giovannetti, S. Lloyd & L. Maccone (2004), *Science*; LIGO Scientific Collaboration (2019), *Phys. Rev. Lett.*

#### `EX-10` — Metrological Traceability & Fundamental Constants
- **Category:** Measurement, Metrology & Precision
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Pragmatic Lab Calibrations` | `Periodic Audit Standards` | `Fundamental-Constant Redefinition Ideal`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should everyday laboratory physics rely on practical artifact/factory calibrations, or push toward self-calibrating quantum standards tied directly to fixed fundamental constants?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Pragmatic Lab Calibrations:* Spend time on new physics, not chasing extra metrological digits beyond what the experiment's error budget actually needs.
  - *Periodic Audit Standards:* Maintain disciplined calibration chains with regular blind reference checks so lab results remain reliable across years.
  - *Fundamental-Constant Redefinition Ideal:* Anchoring every unit to quantum invariants ($h, e, c, k_B, \Delta\nu_{\text{Cs}}$) eliminates drift forever and unites metrology with fundamental physics.
- **Primary citations:** BIPM (2019), SI redefinition; E. Tiesinga et al. (2021), "CODATA Recommended Values of the Fundamental Physical Constants: 2018," *Rev. Mod. Phys.*

---

### Category 3: Instrumentation, Detectors & Hardware Craft

#### `EX-11` — Home-Built Apparatus vs. Turnkey Commercial Instruments
- **Category:** Instrumentation, Detectors & Hardware Craft
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Build Everything In-House` | `Custom Sensor + Commercial Backbone` | `Turnkey Modular Stack`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Does building your own electronics, lasers, and vacuum chambers foster irreplaceable intuition, or waste years reinventing gear that vendors already perfected?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Build Everything In-House:* You only truly understand the systematic bugs of an instrument you designed, machined, wired, and debugged yourself (Rutherford "string and sealing wax" ethos).
  - *Custom Sensor + Commercial Backbone:* Buy standard power supplies, digitizers, and pumps, and concentrate custom engineering on the critical sensing element.
  - *Turnkey Modular Stack:* Reliable commercial hardware lets physicists focus on scientific questions and reproducible protocols rather than endless hardware maintenance.
- **Primary citations:** P. Galison (1997), *Image and Logic*; practitioner experimental-physics culture ("string and sealing wax" tradition associated with E. Rutherford's Cavendish Laboratory).

#### `EX-12` — Analog Front-End Craft vs. Digitize-Early FPGA Processing
- **Category:** Instrumentation, Detectors & Hardware Craft
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Ultra-Low-Noise Analog Purity` | `Balanced Analog & DSP` | `Digitize Immediately in FPGA`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Where should signal conditioning live—in meticulously crafted discrete analog preamps and filters, or in high-speed ADCs feeding reconfigurable FPGA pipelines?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Ultra-Low-Noise Analog Purity:* No digital algorithm can recover information destroyed by a noisy front-end, aliasing, or poor impedance matching.
  - *Balanced Analog & DSP:* Keep the first amplification stage analog-pristine, then transition cleanly to digital filtering, lock-in, and pulse-shape analysis.
  - *Digitize Immediately in FPGA:* Streaming raw digitized waveforms into programmable logic enables flexible triggers, zero analog drift, and lossless offline reprocessing.
- **Primary citations:** P. Horowitz & W. Hill (2015), *The Art of Electronics* (3rd ed.); modern experimental DAQ/FPGA literature (practitioner engineering convention).

#### `EX-13` — Detector Design Priority Compass
- **Category:** Instrumentation, Detectors & Hardware Craft
- **Geometry:** `quad-compass`
- **Poles:** `Ultimate Sensitivity & Low Noise` | `Environmental & Radiation Hardness` | `Timing & Spatial Resolution` | `Scalable Cost-Effectiveness`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Every detector architecture trades off competing engineering constraints; which quality do you refuse to compromise?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Ultimate Sensitivity & Low Noise:* Push down to single quanta, sub-eV thresholds, and thermal/quantum noise floors above all else.
  - *Environmental & Radiation Hardness:* A brilliant detector that degrades after one month of beam exposure or thermal cycling is useless in the field.
  - *Timing & Spatial Resolution:* Picosecond timing and micron-scale tracking disentangle pileup and reconstruct kinematics cleanly.
  - *Scalable Cost-Effectiveness:* Coverage and mass win rare-event searches; modular, affordable channels outperform boutique one-off prototypes.
- **Primary citations:** G. F. Knoll (2010), *Radiation Detection and Measurement* (4th ed.); C. Grupen & B. Shwartz (2008), *Particle Detectors* (2nd ed.).

#### `EX-14` — Hardware Builders vs. Data Analysts Prestige
- **Category:** Instrumentation, Detectors & Hardware Craft
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Instrument Builders Are the Soul` | `Co-Equal Partnership` | `Physics Analysis Leads the Field`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Within experimental physics, should career recognition and leadership favor those who invent and commission detectors, or those who extract headline physics results from the data?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Instrument Builders Are the Soul:* New detectors create entire fields of physics; without hardware pioneers, analysts have nothing to analyze.
  - *Co-Equal Partnership:* Great experiments fuse deep hardware intuition with razor-sharp statistical analysis—neither succeeds without the other.
  - *Physics Analysis Leads the Field:* Instruments are means to an end; interpreting nature and solving statistical/systematic puzzles is the core scientific contribution.
- **Primary citations:** P. Galison (1997), *Image and Logic*; G. Charpak (1992), Nobel Lecture; K. Knorr Cetina (1999), *Epistemic Cultures*.

#### `EX-15` — Local In-House Labs vs. National User Facilities
- **Category:** Instrumentation, Detectors & Hardware Craft
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Always-On Local Laboratory` | `Hybrid Lab-Plus-Beamtime` | `Shared Flagship User Facilities`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should experimentalists optimize around self-contained university labs with 24/7 iteration, or organize research around scheduled beamtime at synchrotrons, XFELs, and neutron sources?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Always-On Local Laboratory:* Continuous, unhurried access to your own apparatus lets you follow anomalies immediately rather than waiting six months for a beamline proposal.
  - *Hybrid Lab-Plus-Beamtime:* Develop and screen samples in-house, then take only your sharpest, best-characterized questions to flagship facilities.
  - *Shared Flagship User Facilities:* No single university can match the brilliance, flux, or specialized end-stations of national accelerators and light sources.
- **Primary citations:** C. Westfall (2008), "Retooling for the Future: Launching the Advanced Light Source at Lawrence's Laboratory," *Hist. Stud. Nat. Sci.*; modern user-facility practice.

#### `EX-16` — Extreme-Environment Engineering Profile
- **Category:** Instrumentation, Detectors & Hardware Craft
- **Geometry:** `star-radar`
- **Poles:** `Ultra-High Vacuum` | `Millikelvin Cryogenics` | `Ultra-High Magnetic Fields` | `Seismic & Vibration Isolation` | `Deep-Underground Low Background`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Which extreme experimental frontier most defines your ideal laboratory craft and technical mastery?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Ultra-High Vacuum:* Mastering surface cleanliness, baking, and $10^{-12}\text{ Torr}$ pressures to isolate atoms and pristine interfaces.
  - *Millikelvin Cryogenics:* Operating dilution refrigerators and superconducting circuits where thermal excitations vanish.
  - *Ultra-High Magnetic Fields:* Generating tens of tesla (continuous or pulsed) to expose Landau levels and exotic quantum phases.
  - *Seismic & Vibration Isolation:* Silencing Earth's microseismic hum to measure displacements smaller than a proton's radius.
  - *Deep-Underground Low Background:* Building radiopure detectors beneath kilometers of rock to listen for single neutrinos or dark-matter recoils.
- **Primary citations:** LIGO Scientific Collaboration (2015), *Class. Quantum Grav.*; F. Pobell (2007), *Matter and Methods at Low Temperatures*; underground rare-event experimental literature.

---

### Category 4: Data Analysis, Blinding & Statistical Standards

#### `EX-17` — Discovery Threshold Discipline ($5\sigma$ vs. Flexible)
- **Category:** Data Analysis, Blinding & Statistical Standards
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Report 2σ–3σ Tensions Early` | `Context-Scaled Threshold` | `Ironclad 5σ Standard`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should experimentalists maintain a rigid $5\sigma$ ($p \approx 3\times 10^{-7}$) bar before announcing a discovery, or adapt thresholds to prior plausibility and look-elsewhere effects?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Report 2σ–3σ Tensions Early:* Sharing modest excesses openly lets independent teams verify or refute them swiftly instead of hiding hints in private memos.
  - *Context-Scaled Threshold:* A single threshold makes no sense across all experiments; extraordinary new-physics claims need $5\sigma+$ while expected Standard Model processes need less.
  - *Ironclad 5σ Standard:* History shows $3\sigma\text{–}4\sigma$ bumps evaporate constantly from look-elsewhere effects and underestimated systematics; $5\sigma$ protects physics' credibility.
- **Primary citations:** L. Lyons (2013), "Discovering the Significance of $5\sigma$," *arXiv:1310.1284*; R. D. Cousins (2017), "The Jeffreys–Lindley Paradox and Discovery Criteria in High Energy Physics," *Synthese*.

#### `EX-18` — Blind Analysis Mandate
- **Category:** Data Analysis, Blinding & Statistical Standards
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Unblinded Exploratory Intuition` | `Signal-Box Blinding` | `Strict Hidden-Offset / Salted Blinding`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** To prevent subconscious confirmation bias ("stopping when the answer matches the PDG value"), how strictly should analysts hide the final result until all cuts and systematics are frozen?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Unblinded Exploratory Intuition:* Looking at real data early catches subtle detector glitches and calibration failures that rigid blinding can freeze into a flawed pipeline.
  - *Signal-Box Blinding:* Mask the signal region while tuning backgrounds and systematics on sidebands, balancing bias protection with detector diagnostics.
  - *Strict Hidden-Offset / Salted Blinding:* Human confirmation bias is pervasive even among Nobel laureates; hidden offsets or salted fake signals are mandatory for credible precision claims.
- **Primary citations:** J. R. Klein & A. Roodman (2005), "Blind Analysis in Nuclear and Particle Physics," *Annu. Rev. Nucl. Part. Sci.*; R. MacCoun & S. Perlmutter (2015), "Blind Analysis: Hide Results to Seek the Truth," *Nature*.

#### `EX-19` — Statistical Inference Paradigm in Experimental Physics
- **Category:** Data Analysis, Blinding & Statistical Standards
- **Geometry:** `triangle`
- **Poles:** `Frequentist Profile Likelihood & CLs` | `Bayesian Posteriors & Priors` | `Feldman–Cousins Unified Intervals`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** When quoting limits, discoveries, and nuisance-parameter uncertainties, which statistical machinery gives the most honest report of what the detector actually measured?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Frequentist Profile Likelihood & CLs:* Coverage guarantees and the $CL_s$ prescription prevent claiming false exclusions from downward background fluctuations without injecting subjective priors.
  - *Bayesian Posteriors & Priors:* Only Bayes' theorem answers what physicists actually want—the probability of a parameter or model given the data—and marginalizes systematics coherently.
  - *Feldman–Cousins Unified Intervals:* Unified likelihood-ratio ordering smoothly transitions between upper limits and two-sided intervals without "flip-flopping" bias.
- **Primary citations:** G. J. Feldman & R. D. Cousins (1998), *Phys. Rev. D*; A. L. Read (2002), *J. Phys. G*; G. Cowan et al. (2011), *Eur. Phys. J. C*; R. D. Cousins (1995), *Am. J. Phys.*

#### `EX-20` — Data-Driven Backgrounds vs. Full Detector Monte Carlo
- **Category:** Data Analysis, Blinding & Statistical Standards
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Pure Data-Driven Sidebands` | `Control-Region-Tuned MC` | `Full First-Principles Detector MC`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** How heavily should an analysis trust full Geant4/instrument Monte Carlo simulations to predict backgrounds versus deriving them directly from control regions in real data?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Pure Data-Driven Sidebands:* Simulations always miss some subtle cable, reflection, or cross-section tail; let the real detector measure its own background via sidebands and ABCD methods.
  - *Control-Region-Tuned MC:* Anchor simulation shapes and normalizations to real control datasets so MC interpolates only where data cannot reach.
  - *Full First-Principles Detector MC:* Modern microphysical transport codes capture complex geometric acceptances and correlated efficiencies impossible to infer from sidebands alone.
- **Primary citations:** S. Agostinelli et al. (2003), "Geant4—A Simulation Toolkit," *Nucl. Instrum. Methods A*; collider & astroparticle analysis methodology.

#### `EX-21` — Machine Learning in Triggers & Event Reconstruction
- **Category:** Data Analysis, Blinding & Statistical Standards
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Physics-Explicit Cut-Based` | `Interpretable Validated BDTs/ML` | `End-to-End Deep Learning on Raw Hits`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should physicists preserve human-interpretable cuts on physical observables, or unleash deep neural networks directly on low-level detector waveforms and hits?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Physics-Explicit Cut-Based:* Transparent cuts on invariant mass, isolation, and pulse shape make systematic uncertainties and simulation mismodeling immediately visible.
  - *Interpretable Validated BDTs/ML:* Multivariate classifiers boosted on calibrated physical features capture correlations while staying auditable against control samples.
  - *End-to-End Deep Learning on Raw Hits:* Graph neural networks and transformers operating on raw detector geometry extract subtle phase-space structure human-engineered variables throw away.
- **Primary citations:** D. Guest, K. Cranmer & D. Whiteson (2018), "Deep Learning and Its Application to LHC Physics," *Annu. Rev. Nucl. Part. Sci.*; G. Karagiorgi et al. (2022), *Nat. Rev. Phys.*

#### `EX-22` — First Reaction to a Headline Experimental Anomaly
- **Category:** Data Analysis, Blinding & Statistical Standards
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Assume Loose Cable / Hidden Systematic` | `Disciplined Agnosticism` | `Treat as New-Physics Lead`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** When a respected experiment reports a startling $3.5\sigma\text{–}4.5\sigma$ discrepancy with established physics, what is your default epistemic posture?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Assume Loose Cable / Hidden Systematic:* From faster-than-light neutrinos to BICEP2 dust, unexpected anomalies almost always trace back to unmodeled hardware or foreground systematics.
  - *Disciplined Agnosticism:* Neither dismiss nor celebrate; map out the exact independent cross-checks that would confirm or kill the effect.
  - *Treat as New-Physics Lead:* Hyper-skepticism risks ignoring genuine cracks in the Standard Model; anomalies deserve vigorous experimental and theoretical follow-up.
- **Primary citations:** A. Franklin (2013), *Shifting Standards: Experiments in Particle Physics in the Twentieth Century*; OPERA Collaboration (2012) fiber-optic cable correction; BICEP2/Keck & Planck Joint Analysis (2015), *Phys. Rev. Lett.*

---

### Category 5: Laboratory Practice & Experimental Craft

#### `EX-23` — Experimental Troubleshooting Ethos
- **Category:** Laboratory Practice & Experimental Craft
- **Geometry:** `xy-switchbox`
- **Poles:** `X: First-Principles Modeling ↔ Empirical Swap-and-Isolate` | `Y: Solo Deep Focus ↔ Collaborative Bench Debugging`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** When an apparatus mysteriously loses lock or develops a 60 Hz spur at 2 a.m., how do you hunt the bug?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *First-Principles Modeling (X-Low):* Stop touching knobs, draw the circuit or optical path, and derive what physical mechanism can produce the symptom.
  - *Empirical Swap-and-Isolate (X-High):* Bisect the signal chain rapidly by swapping cables, terminators, and modules until the culprit confesses.
  - *Solo Deep Focus (Y-Low):* Quiet, systematic solo control ensures only one variable changes at a time without conflicting edits.
  - *Collaborative Bench Debugging (Y-High):* A fresh pair of eyes immediately spots the unpowered supply or blind assumption you stared past for three hours.
- **Primary citations:** Practitioner laboratory folklore & craft culture; P. Horowitz & W. Hill (2015), *The Art of Electronics*.

#### `EX-24` — Hands-On Alignment vs. Closed-Loop Automation
- **Category:** Laboratory Practice & Experimental Craft
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Tactile Manual Mastery` | `Semi-Automated Assist` | `Autonomous Feedback Control`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should physicists pride themselves on manually walking optical beams and tuning cryostats by feel, or automate alignment and locking completely?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Tactile Manual Mastery:* Direct hands-on tuning builds physical intuition for hysteresis, thermal drift, and alignment sensitivity that automated scripts obscure.
  - *Semi-Automated Assist:* Learn the physics manually during commissioning, then script repetitive scans and overnight stabilization.
  - *Autonomous Feedback Control:* Closed-loop servos, automated piezo-alignment, and Bayesian optimizer loops keep complex experiments locked 24/7 with superior repeatability.
- **Primary citations:** Practitioner AMO, optics, and cryogenics lab culture; LIGO interferometric lock-acquisition literature (Staley et al. 2014, *Class. Quantum Grav.*).

#### `EX-25` — Laboratory Documentation & Reproducibility Discipline
- **Category:** Laboratory Practice & Experimental Craft
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Ink-on-Paper Lab Notebook` | `Hybrid Notebook & Git Logs` | `Full Digital ELN & Auto-Metadata`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** How should experimentalists record daily apparatus tweaks, calibration runs, and raw data provenance?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Ink-on-Paper Lab Notebook:* Fast sketches, pasted oscilloscope traces, and chronological ink entries remain unbreakable, frictionless, and readable decades later.
  - *Hybrid Notebook & Git Logs:* Keep creative bench thoughts on paper while version-controlling DAQ scripts, configs, and analysis notebooks in Git.
  - *Full Digital ELN & Auto-Metadata:* Automatically stamping every data file with instrument state, software commit hash, and environmental telemetry eliminates irreproducible "mystery runs."
- **Primary citations:** M. D. Wilkinson et al. (2016), "The FAIR Guiding Principles for Scientific Data Management and Stewardship," *Sci. Data*; practitioner lab convention.

#### `EX-26` — Sample & Target Preparation Ownership
- **Category:** Laboratory Practice & Experimental Craft
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Acquire from Dedicated Growers` | `Tight Co-Design Partnership` | `Fabricate Every Sample In-House`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** In condensed-matter, nuclear, and AMO experiments, should the measurement team fabricate its own crystals, films, and targets, or rely on specialized materials collaborators?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Acquire from Dedicated Growers:* World-class crystal growth and nanofabrication are full-time disciplines; partnering with master growers beats mediocre in-house samples.
  - *Tight Co-Design Partnership:* Pair dedicated materials synthesis with fast measurement feedback loops so sample quality and device geometry co-evolve.
  - *Fabricate Every Sample In-House:* Controlling growth, cleanliness, and interfaces under your own roof is the only way to diagnose sample-dependent anomalies rapidly.
- **Primary citations:** Practitioner condensed-matter & nuclear-targetry subdisciplinary convention (e.g., Paul Canfield's "solution growth" philosophy in *Rep. Prog. Phys.* 2020).

#### `EX-27` — Noise & Grounding Hygiene ("Know Thy Ground Loops")
- **Category:** Laboratory Practice & Experimental Craft
- **Geometry:** `monopole-slider`
- **Poles:** `Obsessive Front-End Noise & Grounding Discipline`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** How uncompromising should a lab be about star grounding, optical isolation, RF shielding, and thermal stabilization before taking real measurements?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Obsessive Front-End Noise & Grounding Discipline:* Never average away noise you can eliminate physically; clean grounding, shielding, and vibration hygiene pay compound dividends on every run.
- **Primary citations:** H. W. Ott (2009), *Electromagnetic Compatibility Engineering*; P. Horowitz & W. Hill (2015), *The Art of Electronics*.

---

### Category 6: Collaboration, Scale & Experimental Culture

#### `EX-28` — Big Science vs. Tabletop Small Science
- **Category:** Collaboration, Scale & Experimental Culture
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Agile PI-Led Tabletop` | `Mid-Scale Consortium (10–100)` | `Multi-Thousand-Author Flagship`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** What organizational scale produces the healthiest, most creative, and most productive experimental physics?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Agile PI-Led Tabletop:* Small teams of 3–8 physicists pivot in weeks, let graduate students own an entire experiment end-to-end, and foster fearless creativity.
  - *Mid-Scale Consortium (10–100):* Mid-scale experiments combine serious technical firepower with flat governance where every member still knows the whole apparatus.
  - *Multi-Thousand-Author Flagship:* Only global mega-collaborations can build and operate instruments at the energy, cosmic, and gravitational frontiers.
- **Primary citations:** A. M. Weinberg (1961), "Impact of Large-Scale Science on the United States," *Science*; P. Galison & B. Hevly, eds. (1992), *Big Science: The Growth of Large-Scale Research*.

#### `EX-29` — Authorship & Credit in Large Collaborations
- **Category:** Collaboration, Scale & Experimental Culture
- **Geometry:** `triangle`
- **Poles:** `Strict Alphabetical Equal Credit` | `Granular CRediT & Role Attribution` | `Small-Team Earned Lead Authorship`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** How should large experiments assign authorship credit so detector builders, shift-takers, software engineers, and lead analysts are all treated fairly?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Strict Alphabetical Equal Credit:* Alphabetical collaboration-wide authorship protects essential hardware and operations builders from being sidelined by headline analysts.
  - *Granular CRediT & Role Attribution:* Standardized contributor-role metadata lets every member receive credit while transparently showing who built, ran, and analyzed the paper.
  - *Small-Team Earned Lead Authorship:* Ordering authors by direct intellectual contribution gives early-career researchers clear visibility on the job market.
- **Primary citations:** J. P. Birnholtz (2006), "What Does It Mean to Be an Author? The Intersection of Credit, Contribution, and Collaboration in Science," *J. Am. Soc. Inf. Sci. Technol.*; A. Brand et al. (2015), "Beyond Authorship: Attribution, Contribution, Collaboration, and Credit," *Learn. Publ.*

#### `EX-30` — Raw Data Openness vs. Collaboration Embargo
- **Category:** Collaboration, Scale & Experimental Culture
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Immediate Public Raw Data` | `Timed Embargo Then Open Release` | `Collaboration-Gated Access`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should publicly funded experiments release raw detector data immediately for external reanalysis, or restrict access until the builders have extracted core results and documented caveats?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Immediate Public Raw Data:* Taxpayers funded the instrument; open raw data accelerates discovery, invites fresh methods, and catches groupthink.
  - *Timed Embargo Then Open Release:* Give hardware builders a fair 1–3 year window to publish primary results and calibrate systematics, then release well-documented open datasets.
  - *Collaboration-Gated Access:* Uncalibrated raw data in external hands invites spurious discoveries from misunderstood detector artifacts that waste community effort to refute.
- **Primary citations:** CERN Open Data Policy (2020); Gravitational Wave Open Science Center (GWOSC, Abbott et al. 2021, *SoftwareX*).

#### `EX-31` — Commissioning Risk & Schedule Philosophy
- **Category:** Collaboration, Scale & Experimental Culture
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Ship Early & Iterate Live` | `Staged Commissioning Gates` | `Exhaustive Pre-Run Qualification`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should experimentalists get an imperfect prototype taking data quickly and refine it iteratively, or qualify every subsystem exhaustively before first light or launch?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Ship Early & Iterate Live:* Real beam or real vacuum teaches you ten times faster than endless bench simulations; get photons on the detector fast.
  - *Staged Commissioning Gates:* Use milestone engineering runs to validate subsystems progressively while keeping clear go/no-go safety gates.
  - *Exhaustive Pre-Run Qualification:* On space missions, deep-underground tanks, or sealed cryostats, one missed failure mode costs years; qualify everything ruthlessly upfront.
- **Primary citations:** NASA Technology Readiness Level (TRL) engineering culture (Mankins 1995); practitioner lab vs. space-instrumentation divide.

#### `EX-32` — Independent Duplicate Analysis Pipelines
- **Category:** Collaboration, Scale & Experimental Culture
- **Geometry:** `monopole-slider`
- **Poles:** `Mandatory Two-Team Independent Analysis`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should major experimental measurements require two independent teams writing separate analysis code from scratch before unblinding and publication?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Mandatory Two-Team Independent Analysis:* Silent coding bugs, sign flips, and cut artifacts routinely survive single-team review; independent re-implementation is the cheapest insurance against catastrophe.
- **Primary citations:** Practice documented in ATLAS/CMS, LIGO, Event Horizon Telescope (EHT Collaboration 2019, *ApJL*), and precision-metrology reviews.

#### `EX-33` — Experimental Aesthetic Ideal
- **Category:** Collaboration, Scale & Experimental Culture
- **Geometry:** `diamond`
- **Poles:** `Elegant Null-Test Simplicity` | `Monumental Engineering Triumph` | `Razor-Sharp Spectral Peak` | `Serendipitous First Light`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Which kind of experimental moment represents the pinnacle of beauty in physics?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Elegant Null-Test Simplicity:* A clean tabletop symmetry test—like Eötvös's torsion balance or Pound–Rebka—where ingenuity defeats brute force.
  - *Monumental Engineering Triumph:* Thousands of precision subsystems harmonizing across kilometers to hear two black holes collide.
  - *Razor-Sharp Spectral Peak:* Watching a narrow, undeniable resonance rise cleanly out of flat background to ten significant figures.
  - *Serendipitous First Light:* Pointing a brand-new instrument at nature and immediately seeing an unexpected signal nobody predicted.
- **Primary citations:** R. P. Crease (2002), *The Prism and the Pendulum: The Ten Most Beautiful Experiments in Science*; P. Galison (1997).

#### `EX-34` — Generalist Experimentalist vs. Hyper-Specialist
- **Category:** Collaboration, Scale & Experimental Culture
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Full-Stack Renaissance Experimentalist` | `T-Shaped Specialist` | `Dedicated Subsystem Master`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should an experimental physicist be able to machine parts, solder circuits, write DAQ firmware, and do statistical fits personally, or go extraordinarily deep in one specialty?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Full-Stack Renaissance Experimentalist:* End-to-end command of mechanics, optics, electronics, and statistics lets one spot cross-boundary systematics specialists miss.
  - *T-Shaped Specialist:* Build broad working fluency across the whole apparatus while cultivating world-class depth in one core pillar.
  - *Dedicated Subsystem Master:* Modern frontier experiments are too technically demanding for jacks-of-all-trades; excellence requires specialized career experts.
- **Primary citations:** K. Knorr Cetina (1999), *Epistemic Cultures: How the Sciences Make Knowledge*.

---

### Category 7: Astroparticle, Observational & Multi-Messenger Frontiers

#### `EX-35` — Ground-Based Flagships vs. Space-Based Observatories
- **Category:** Astroparticle, Observational & Multi-Messenger Frontiers
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Adaptive Ground-Based Giants` | `Coordinated Ground + Space Synergy` | `Space-Based Cryo & Zero-Atmosphere Purity`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** With laser-guide-star adaptive optics and massive 30 m apertures on Earth, when is the $10\times$ cost and unserviceability of space telescopes truly justified?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Adaptive Ground-Based Giants:* Ground telescopes deliver enormous collecting areas, upgradeable detectors, and multi-decade lifetimes at a fraction of launch costs.
  - *Coordinated Ground + Space Synergy:* Use space observatories for opaque wavelengths (IR, X-ray, gamma, mHz GW) and ground behemoths for deep spectroscopy and rapid transient follow-up.
  - *Space-Based Cryo & Zero-Atmosphere Purity:* Freedom from atmospheric turbulence, thermal background, and seismic gravity-gradient noise makes space missions irreplaceable for CMB, JWST, and LISA.
- **Primary citations:** National Academies (2021), *Pathways to Discovery in Astronomy and Astrophysics for the 2020s (Astro2020)*; H. W. Babcock (1953), adaptive optics.

#### `EX-36` — Multi-Messenger Cosmic Probe Allegiance
- **Category:** Astroparticle, Observational & Multi-Messenger Frontiers
- **Geometry:** `quad-compass`
- **Poles:** `Electromagnetic Photons (Radio to Gamma)` | `Gravitational-Wave Interferometry` | `High-Energy Neutrino Telescopes` | `Ultra-High-Energy Cosmic Rays`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Which cosmic messenger carries the cleanest, least obscured view into the universe's most violent engines?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Electromagnetic Photons (Radio to Gamma):* Photons provide unmatched angular resolution, rich atomic/molecular spectroscopy, and precision redshift measurements across cosmic history.
  - *Gravitational-Wave Interferometry:* Spacetime ripples pass unimpeded through dense matter, letting us hear black-hole horizons and neutron-star interiors directly.
  - *High-Energy Neutrino Telescopes:* Neutrinos point straight back to hadronic accelerators deep inside opaque cores where photons are absorbed and cosmic rays deflect.
  - *Ultra-High-Energy Cosmic Rays:* Direct samples of nuclei accelerated past $10^{20}\,\text{eV}$ probe particle interactions far beyond any human-built collider.
- **Primary citations:** LIGO/Virgo & Multi-Messenger Partners (2017), "Multi-Messenger Observations of a Binary Neutron Star Merger (GW170817)," *ApJL*; IceCube Collaboration (2018), *Science*.

#### `EX-37` — Deep Pointed Stares vs. Wide-Field Synoptic Surveys
- **Category:** Astroparticle, Observational & Multi-Messenger Frontiers
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Deep Targeted Object Physics` | `Survey Trigger + Deep Follow-Up` | `All-Sky Time-Domain Surveys First`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should observational physics invest mainly in long, ultra-deep pointed exposures on prized targets, or wide-field automated sky-scanning machines generating millions of transient alerts nightly?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Deep Targeted Object Physics:* High-S/N spectroscopy and deep integrations on golden benchmark objects solve physical mechanisms that shallow statistics blur together.
  - *Survey Trigger + Deep Follow-Up:* Let robotic wide-field surveys catch rare kilonovae and fast transients, immediately triggering deep spectroscopic follow-up.
  - *All-Sky Time-Domain Surveys First:* Statistical cosmology, dark energy, and unknown transient populations (FRBs, tidal disruptions) emerge only from unbiased, petabyte-scale sky surveys (SDSS, Rubin/LSST).
- **Primary citations:** D. G. York et al. (2000), *AJ* (SDSS); Ž. Ivezić et al. (2019), *ApJ* (Rubin Observatory/LSST).

#### `EX-38` — Suborbital Balloons & Sounding Rockets vs. Flagship Satellites
- **Category:** Astroparticle, Observational & Multi-Messenger Frontiers
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Fast High-Risk Suborbital Campaigns` | `Balloon Pathfinders to Space Flagships` | `Class-A Full-Sky Space Missions`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should astroparticle experimentalists embrace nimble, student-built stratospheric balloons (BOOMERanG, ANITA, SPIDER) or wait a decade for space-qualified satellites (Planck, Fermi)?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Fast High-Risk Suborbital Campaigns:* Balloons and rockets fly cutting-edge detectors in 2–4 years, train hands-on instrument builders, and tolerate bold experimental risk.
  - *Balloon Pathfinders to Space Flagships:* Mature novel cryogenic and focal-plane sensors on suborbital flights first, then lock in full-sky systematics from L2 orbit.
  - *Class-A Full-Sky Space Missions:* Only years-long, stable full-sky satellite integration eliminates horizon cuts, atmospheric residuals, and patchy coverage.
- **Primary citations:** P. de Bernardis et al. (2000), *Nature* (BOOMERanG); Planck Collaboration (2020), *A&A*.

#### `EX-39` — Dark Matter Search Complementarity Strategy
- **Category:** Astroparticle, Observational & Multi-Messenger Frontiers
- **Geometry:** `quad-compass`
- **Poles:** `Underground Direct Recoil (Xe / Cryo)` | `Axion Haloscopes & Wave Detectors` | `Indirect Astrophysical Annihilation` | `Collider Missing-Energy Production`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** With the classic TeV WIMP window tightly constrained near the neutrino fog, which experimental dark-matter channel holds the strongest discovery promise?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Underground Direct Recoil (Xe / Cryo):* Multi-ton noble liquids and sub-eV cryogenic bolometers remain the cleanest direct proof of galactic dark-matter scattering.
  - *Axion Haloscopes & Wave Detectors:* Quantum-limited microwave cavities and NMR magnetometers open vast unexplored decades of wave-like ultralight dark matter (axions, dark photons).
  - *Indirect Astrophysical Annihilation:* Gamma-ray, cosmic-ray, and neutrino observatories probe dark-matter self-annihilation across dwarf galaxies and galactic centers natively.
  - *Collider Missing-Energy Production:* Producing dark-sector mediators under controlled laboratory kinematics pins down couplings independent of uncertain local halo velocities.
- **Primary citations:** P. Sikivie (1983), *Phys. Rev. Lett.*; M. W. Goodman & E. Witten (1985), *Phys. Rev. D*; J. Billard et al. (2022), *Rep. Prog. Phys.*

#### `EX-40` — Real-Time Low-Latency Alerts vs. Offline Reprocessed Catalogs
- **Category:** Astroparticle, Observational & Multi-Messenger Frontiers
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Seconds-Scale Public Transient Alerts` | `Tiered Alert + Audited Catalog` | `Carefully Calibrated Offline Releases`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should detectors broadcast candidate astrophysical triggers within seconds (risking false alarms) so partner telescopes can slew immediately, or withhold until full offline calibration finishes?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Seconds-Scale Public Transient Alerts:* Early shock breakout and kilonova physics vanish in minutes; fast automated alerts are essential even if occasional retractions happen.
  - *Tiered Alert + Audited Catalog:* Broadcast low-latency alerts with honest false-alarm probabilities, then publish rigorously vetted offline catalogs for precision cosmology.
  - *Carefully Calibrated Offline Releases:* Premature public triggers waste precious telescope hours and pollute literature; precision science belongs to fully calibrated offline pipelines.
- **Primary citations:** LIGO/Virgo Public Alert System (GraceDB); IceCube Real-Time Alert System (Aartsen et al. 2017, *Astropart. Phys.*).

### Category 8: Quantum Control, AMO & Ultrafast Laser Physics

#### `EX-41` — Tabletop Quantum Simulator Platform Preference
- **Category:** Quantum Control, AMO & Ultrafast Laser Physics
- **Geometry:** `triangle`
- **Poles:** `Ultracold Atoms in Optical Lattices` | `Trapped-Ion Spin Chains` | `Programmable Rydberg Optical Tweezers`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Which clean AMO platform offers the most versatile laboratory microscope onto intractable quantum many-body physics?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Ultracold Atoms in Optical Lattices:* Quantum gas microscopes realize clean Fermi- and Bose–Hubbard models with single-site imaging and thousands of itinerant fermions.
  - *Trapped-Ion Spin Chains:* Tunable long-range Ising/XY couplings and near-unit detection fidelity make ion strings unmatched for quantum dynamics and entanglement tomography.
  - *Programmable Rydberg Optical Tweezers:* Arbitrary 2D/3D defect-free atom geometries with strong van der Waals blockade probe quantum spin liquids and gauge theories cleanly.
- **Primary citations:** M. Greiner et al. (2002), *Nature*; I. Bloch, J. Dalibard & S. Nascimbène (2012), *Nat. Phys.*; C. Monroe et al. (2021), *Rev. Mod. Phys.*; A. Browaeys & T. Lahaye (2020), *Nat. Phys.*

#### `EX-42` — Continuous-Wave Precision Spectroscopy vs. Ultrafast Attosecond Pulses
- **Category:** Quantum Control, AMO & Ultrafast Laser Physics
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Ultra-Narrow CW Frequency Combs` | `Dual Frequency-Time Comb Mastery` | `Femtosecond & Attosecond Pump-Probe`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Do you prefer squeezing energy uncertainty ($\Delta E \to 0$) with sub-hertz continuous lasers, or squeezing time uncertainty ($\Delta t \to \text{as}$) to watch electrons tunnel in real time?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Ultra-Narrow CW Frequency Combs:* Narrow-linewidth optical lattices and frequency combs test QED, drift of fundamental constants, and gravitational redshift to $10^{-19}$.
  - *Dual Frequency-Time Comb Mastery:* Optical frequency combs literally unite ultrafast mode-locked pulse trains with ultra-high-resolution frequency-domain metrology (Hänsch, Hall).
  - *Femtosecond & Attosecond Pump-Probe:* High-harmonic generation and XFELs directly capture non-equilibrium electron correlation, light-induced superconductivity, and photoionization delays.
- **Primary citations:** T. W. Hänsch (2006) & J. L. Hall (2006), Nobel Lectures (*Rev. Mod. Phys.*); P. Agostini, F. Krausz & A. L'Huillier (2023 Nobel Prize in Physics).

#### `EX-43` — Cavity QED & Collective Strong Coupling vs. Free-Space Isolation
- **Category:** Quantum Control, AMO & Ultrafast Laser Physics
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Clean Free-Space Atom/Photon Beams` | `Controlled Cavity Enhancement` | `Deep Strong Coupling & Cavity Engineering`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should quantum-optics experiments keep emitters isolated in free space to avoid wall shifts and cavity noise, or enclose them in high-finesse cavities to engineer strong light–matter interactions?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Clean Free-Space Atom/Photon Beams:* Free-space optical traps and atomic fountains eliminate surface patch potentials, blackbody wall proximity, and cavity mode distortion.
  - *Controlled Cavity Enhancement:* Optical and microwave cavities boost readout speed, photon collection efficiency, and spin squeezing without sacrificing coherence.
  - *Deep Strong Coupling & Cavity Engineering:* Strong vacuum coupling creates polaritons, photon-mediated long-range interactions, and quantum non-demolition photon counting (Haroche).
- **Primary citations:** S. Haroche (2013), "Controlling Photons in a Box and Exploring the Quantum to Classical Boundary," *Rev. Mod. Phys.*; H. Ritsch et al. (2013), *Rev. Mod. Phys.*

#### `EX-44` — Single-Shot Quantum Trajectory Readout vs. High-Flux Ensemble Averaging
- **Category:** Quantum Control, AMO & Ultrafast Laser Physics
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `High-SNR Macroscopic Ensembles` | `Cross-Checked Single & Ensemble` | `Single-Quantum Projective & Trajectory Control`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should precision experiments leverage $\sqrt{N}$ signal-to-noise from $10^{12}$ atoms/spins, or isolate single ions, electrons, and superconducting circuits for deterministic quantum-jump tracking?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *High-SNR Macroscopic Ensembles:* Vapor-cell SERF magnetometers and macroscopic NMR ensembles achieve unmatched absolute sensitivity per unit time.
  - *Cross-Checked Single & Ensemble:* Ensembles win raw sensitivity while single-quantum control eliminates inhomogeneous broadening and density shifts.
  - *Single-Quantum Projective & Trajectory Control:* Trapping a single electron ($g-2$) or watching real-time quantum jumps with feedback eliminates collisional shifts entirely (Dehmelt, Wineland).
- **Primary citations:** H. Dehmelt (1990), *Rev. Mod. Phys.*; D. J. Wineland (2013), *Rev. Mod. Phys.*; Z. K. Minev et al. (2019), *Nature* (catching and reversing a quantum jump).

#### `EX-45` — Laser-Plasma Wakefield vs. Conventional RF Acceleration
- **Category:** Quantum Control, AMO & Ultrafast Laser Physics
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Proven Superconducting RF Cavities` | `Hybrid Plasma-Booster Staging` | `Tabletop Laser-Plasma Wakefield Revolution`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Can ultra-high-gradient plasma wakefield accelerators ($\sim 100\,\text{GV/m}$) shrink multi-kilometer colliders and XFELs onto university lab tables, or will beam emittance and wall-plug efficiency keep RF cavities dominant?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Proven Superconducting RF Cavities:* High repetition rate, low energy spread, and exquisite luminosity control make superconducting RF the only credible workhorse for precision physics.
  - *Hybrid Plasma-Booster Staging:* Use conventional RF injectors for pristine beam quality and plasma stages as compact energy boosters or medical/imaging light sources.
  - *Tabletop Laser-Plasma Wakefield Revolution:* Breaking the RF metallic-breakdown limit by three orders of magnitude is the only sustainable path beyond 100 km civil-engineering footprints.
- **Primary citations:** T. Tajima & J. M. Dawson (1979), *Phys. Rev. Lett.*; E. Esarey, C. B. Schroeder & W. P. Leemans (2009), *Rev. Mod. Phys.*

#### `EX-46` — Cold-Atom Matter-Wave Interferometry vs. Optical Interferometry
- **Category:** Quantum Control, AMO & Ultrafast Laser Physics
- **Geometry:** `linear-sliders`
- **Poles:** `Laser Optical Interferometry` | `Atom Matter-Wave Interferometry`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** How strongly do you bet on laser-mirror interferometers (LIGO-class) versus free-falling atomic de Broglie matter-wave interferometers for gravimetry, dark matter, and mid-band gravitational waves?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Laser Optical Interferometry:* Enormous circulating optical power, squeezed light, and mature suspended-mirror technology provide unrivaled strain sensitivity over wide frequency bands.
  - *Atom Matter-Wave Interferometry:* Free-falling atoms act as pristine inertial proof masses immune to mechanical suspension thermal noise—ideal for gravimetry and 0.01–1 Hz gravitational waves.
- **Primary citations:** M. Kasevich & S. Chu (1991), *Phys. Rev. Lett.*; P. W. Graham et al. (2013), *Phys. Rev. Lett.* (atom interferometry for GW).

### Category 9: Accelerator, Beam & Condensed-Matter Probes

#### `EX-47` — Next Flagship Collider: Precision Lepton vs. Discovery Hadron vs. Muon
- **Category:** Accelerator, Beam & Condensed-Matter Probes
- **Geometry:** `triangle`
- **Poles:** `Clean e+e- Higgs Factory` | `100 TeV Hadron Discovery Machine` | `Compact High-Energy Muon Collider`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** What should follow the High-Luminosity LHC as particle physics's next multi-decade flagship accelerator?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Clean e+e- Higgs Factory:* Point-like electron–positron collisions measure Higgs and electroweak couplings to sub-percent precision with crystal-clear backgrounds (FCC-ee, ILC, CEPC).
  - *100 TeV Hadron Discovery Machine:* Only a super-high-energy proton collider directly blasts open the multi-TeV discovery frontier and measures the Higgs self-coupling directly (FCC-hh).
  - *Compact High-Energy Muon Collider:* Heavy muons suppress synchrotron radiation, delivering multi-TeV point-like collisions inside a compact, far cheaper, lower-power ring if cooling succeeds.
- **Primary citations:** European Strategy Group (2020), *Update of the European Strategy for Particle Physics*; P5 Report (2023), *Exploring the Quantum Universe*; C. Accettura et al. (2023), *Eur. Phys. J. C* (Muon Collider).

#### `EX-48` — Real-Space Scanning Probes vs. Reciprocal-Space Scattering
- **Category:** Accelerator, Beam & Condensed-Matter Probes
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Atomic Real-Space STM / STEM` | `Complementary Real + k-Space Fusion` | `Bulk Reciprocal-Space ARPES / Neutron / X-Ray`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** To uncover the true physics of quantum materials, do you trust atomic-resolution surface microscopes (STM/AFM) or momentum-resolved bulk scattering (neutrons, X-rays, ARPES)?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Atomic Real-Space STM / STEM:* Imaging individual impurities, vortices, and nanoscale electronic puddles directly reveals local heterogeneity that spatially averaged scattering smears out.
  - *Complementary Real + k-Space Fusion:* Fourier-transform quasiparticle interference (FT-STM) paired with bulk neutron/ARPES proves surface states match bulk excitations.
  - *Bulk Reciprocal-Space ARPES / Neutron / X-Ray:* Cleaved surfaces reconstruct and distort symmetry; bulk inelastic neutron and resonant X-ray scattering measure true thermodynamic dispersions unambiguously.
- **Primary citations:** G. Binnig & H. Rohrer (1987), *Rev. Mod. Phys.*; A. Damascelli, Z. Hussain & Z.-X. Shen (2003), *Rev. Mod. Phys.* (ARPES); J. C. Davis & D.-H. Lee (2013), *PNAS*.

#### `EX-49` — Proving an Exotic Condensed-Matter Phase: Transport vs. Thermodynamics
- **Category:** Accelerator, Beam & Condensed-Matter Probes
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Electrical & Thermal Transport Leads` | `Multi-Probe Consilience Required` | `Bulk Thermodynamics & Meissner Proof Only`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** How skeptical should experimentalists be of dramatic electrical resistance drops or conductance peaks before bulk specific heat, magnetization, or thermal Hall signatures confirm a new phase?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Electrical & Thermal Transport Leads:* Quantized Hall plateaus, zero-resistance drops, and nonlocal edge transport are the sharpest, fastest discovery probes of topological and superconducting phases.
  - *Multi-Probe Consilience Required:* Pair transport with optical, tunneling, and magnetization checks so contact artifacts or filamentary shorts are caught immediately.
  - *Bulk Thermodynamics & Meissner Proof Only:* Resistance can drop to zero from a tiny percolating impurity filament; only bulk heat capacity, entropy jumps, and full Meissner diamagnetism prove a true thermodynamic phase.
- **Primary citations:** W. Meissner & R. Ochsenfeld (1933), *Naturwissenschaften*; J. E. Hirsch & F. Marsiglio (2021) & superconductivity verification standards; condensed-matter experimental consensus.

#### `EX-50` — Synchrotron X-Rays vs. Neutron Scattering vs. Cryo-EM
- **Category:** Accelerator, Beam & Condensed-Matter Probes
- **Geometry:** `triangle`
- **Poles:** `High-Brilliance Synchrotron / XFEL X-Rays` | `Magnetic & Light-Atom Neutron Beams` | `Direct Real-Space Cryo-EM & 4D-STEM`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Each flagship structural probe sees different facets of matter—which beamline capability is most indispensable to modern experimental physics?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *High-Brilliance Synchrotron / XFEL X-Rays:* Coherent diffraction-limited X-rays deliver nanometer focusing, elemental specificity (RIXS/XAS), and femtosecond movie snapshots.
  - *Magnetic & Light-Atom Neutron Beams:* Neutrons scatter off nuclei and magnetic moments directly—irreplaceable for locating hydrogen, phonons, magnons, and quantum spin liquids.
  - *Direct Real-Space Cryo-EM & 4D-STEM:* Electrons interact $10^4\times$ more strongly than X-rays, resolving atomic structures of uncrystallized complexes, interfaces, and local electric fields directly.
- **Primary citations:** G. L. Squires (2012), *Introduction to the Theory of Thermal Neutron Scattering*; J. Dubochet, J. Frank & R. Henderson (2017 Nobel Prize, Cryo-EM); P. Willmott (2019), *An Introduction to Synchrotron Radiation*.

#### `EX-51` — Steady-State DC High Fields vs. Destructive Pulsed Mega-Fields
- **Category:** Accelerator, Beam & Condensed-Matter Probes
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Quiet Continuous DC Magnets (≤45 T)` | `Non-Destructive Millisecond Pulses (60–100 T)` | `Single-Shot Destructive Flux Compression (>100 T)`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** To suppress superconductivity or reach the quantum limit in solids, how extreme should magnetic-field instrumentation push?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Quiet Continuous DC Magnets (≤45 T):* Stable DC fields allow long signal averaging, dilution-fridge thermalization, NMR, and delicate torque/calorimetry impossible during fast pulses.
  - *Non-Destructive Millisecond Pulses (60–100 T):* Capacitor-bank pulsed coils reach 100 T repeatedly in compact labs, mapping full upper-critical-field and quantum-oscillation phase diagrams.
  - *Single-Shot Destructive Flux Compression (>100 T):* Reaching 300–1000 T—even if the coil explodes in microseconds—exposes ultrahigh-field physics accessible nowhere else on Earth.
- **Primary citations:** F. Herlach & N. Miura, eds. (2003), *High Magnetic Fields: Science and Technology*; National High Magnetic Field Laboratory (NHMFL) operational paradigm.

#### `EX-52` — High-Pressure Extreme Matter: Diamond Anvil Cells vs. Dynamic Shock Compression
- **Category:** Accelerator, Beam & Condensed-Matter Probes
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Static Equilibrium Diamond Anvil Cells` | `Laser-Heated & Ramp-Compressed Bridge` | `Ultra-High-Pressure Laser/Z-Pinch Shocks`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** How should experimentalists probe planetary cores, metallic hydrogen, and extreme states of matter at megabar-to-gigabar pressures?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Static Equilibrium Diamond Anvil Cells:* Transparent diamond windows let X-rays, Raman lasers, and electrical leads study well-equilibrated phases cleanly up to several megabars.
  - *Laser-Heated & Ramp-Compressed Bridge:* Quasi-isentropic ramp compression bridges static megabar cells and hot shock states without melting the sample prematurely.
  - *Ultra-High-Pressure Laser/Z-Pinch Shocks:* Giant laser and pulsed-power facilities (NIF, Z Machine) reach tens of megabars to gigabars—directly recreating giant-planet cores and warm dense matter.
- **Primary citations:** H.-K. Mao et al. (2018), *Rev. Mod. Phys.* (Diamond Anvil Cells); B. A. Remington, R. P. Drake & D. D. Ryutov (2006), *Rev. Mod. Phys.*

## 4. Canonical Archetypes (11 Archetypes)

### 1. The "String & Sealing Wax" Tabletop Virtuoso
- **Summary:** Champions agile, PI-led tabletop ingenuity where deep physical intuition and custom-built apparatus rival giant facilities at a fraction of the cost.
- **Primary Thinkers & Literature:** E. Rutherford (Cavendish tradition), R. V. Pound & G. A. Rebka (1960), D. DeMille et al. (2017), R. P. Crease (2002).
- **Stance Profile:**
  - **Anchors High:** `EX-01` (*Autonomous Exploratory Science* / *Iterative Co-Evolution*), `EX-06` (*Ultra-Precision Tabletop & Clocks*), `EX-11` (*Build Everything In-House*), `EX-14` (*Instrument Builders Are the Soul*), `EX-15` (*Always-On Local Laboratory*), `EX-24` (*Tactile Manual Mastery*), `EX-28` (*Agile PI-Led Tabletop*), `EX-29` (*Small-Team Earned Lead Authorship*), `EX-33` (*Elegant Null-Test Simplicity*), `EX-34` (*Full-Stack Renaissance Experimentalist*).
  - **Anchors Low:** `EX-28` (*Multi-Thousand-Author Flagship*), `EX-31` (*Exhaustive Pre-Run Qualification*).

### 2. The Big-Science Collider Strategist
- **Summary:** Orchestrates large-scale, high-rate detector collaborations where statistical rigor, trigger logic, and systematic control extract rare signals from petabytes of collisions.
- **Primary Thinkers & Literature:** S. van der Meer, C. Rubbia, P. Galison's "logic" tradition (1997), ATLAS/CMS/LHC collaboration culture, G. Cowan et al. (2011).
- **Stance Profile:**
  - **Anchors High:** `EX-02` (*High-Rate Statistical Logic*), `EX-06` (*Highest-Energy Colliders*), `EX-13` (*Environmental & Radiation Hardness* & *Timing & Spatial Resolution*), `EX-17` (*Ironclad 5σ Standard*), `EX-18` (*Signal-Box Blinding*), `EX-19` (*Frequentist Profile Likelihood & CLs*), `EX-20` (*Control-Region-Tuned MC*), `EX-21` (*Interpretable Validated BDTs/ML* / *End-to-End Deep Learning*), `EX-28` (*Multi-Thousand-Author Flagship*), `EX-29` (*Strict Alphabetical Equal Credit*), `EX-32` (High *Mandatory Two-Team Independent Analysis*), `EX-33` (*Monumental Engineering Triumph*).
  - **Anchors Low:** `EX-02` (*Golden-Event Direct Image*), `EX-11` (*Build Everything In-House* for everything), `EX-28` (*Agile PI-Led Tabletop*).

### 3. The Quantum Metrologist & Clockmaker
- **Summary:** Pursues ultimate fractional precision via optical clocks, quantum-limited interferometry, reversal symmetries, and fundamental-constant traceability.
- **Primary Thinkers & Literature:** N. F. Ramsey, J. L. Hall, T. W. Hänsch, D. J. Wineland, J. Ye, C. M. Caves (1981), BIPM (2019).
- **Stance Profile:**
  - **Anchors High:** `EX-05` (*Triumph of Precision Craft*), `EX-06` (*Ultra-Precision Tabletop & Clocks*), `EX-07` (*Systematic-Control Obsession*), `EX-08` (*Differential Null & Lock-In Mastery* + *Absolute SI-Traceable Calibration* dual), `EX-09` (High *Commitment to Squeezed & Entangled Metrology*), `EX-10` (*Fundamental-Constant Redefinition Ideal*), `EX-13` (*Ultimate Sensitivity & Low Noise*), `EX-16` (*Ultra-High Vacuum* & *Seismic & Vibration Isolation*), `EX-27` (High *Obsessive Front-End Noise & Grounding Discipline*), `EX-33` (*Razor-Sharp Spectral Peak*).
  - **Anchors Low:** `EX-07` (*Maximize Raw Statistics First*), `EX-10` (*Pragmatic Lab Calibrations*).

### 4. The Master Detector & Instrumentation Builder
- **Summary:** Believes physics leaps forward whenever someone invents a radically better sensor, preamp, or cryo-mechanical architecture—hardware craft is the true engine of discovery.
- **Primary Thinkers & Literature:** G. Charpak (1992), L. W. Alvarez, R. Weiss, P. Horowitz & W. Hill (2015), G. F. Knoll (2010).
- **Stance Profile:**
  - **Anchors High:** `EX-11` (*Build Everything In-House* / *Custom Sensor + Commercial Backbone*), `EX-12` (*Ultra-Low-Noise Analog Purity* or *Balanced Analog & DSP*), `EX-13` (*Ultimate Sensitivity & Low Noise* & *Timing & Spatial Resolution*), `EX-14` (*Instrument Builders Are the Soul*), `EX-16` (High across *Cryogenics*, *UHV*, *Vibration Isolation*), `EX-23` (*First-Principles Modeling* + *Empirical Swap-and-Isolate* mastery), `EX-26` (*Fabricate Every Sample In-House*), `EX-27` (High *Noise & Grounding Hygiene*), `EX-31` (*Staged Commissioning Gates*).
  - **Anchors Low:** `EX-14` (*Physics Analysis Leads the Field*), `EX-11` (*Turnkey Modular Stack*).

### 5. The Blind-Analysis Statistical Purist
- **Summary:** Guards experimental physics against confirmation bias, look-elsewhere traps, and phantom bumps through strict blinding, principled statistics, and ruthless systematic audits.
- **Primary Thinkers & Literature:** L. Lyons (2013), G. J. Feldman & R. D. Cousins (1998), J. R. Klein & A. Roodman (2005), R. MacCoun & S. Perlmutter (2015), A. Franklin (2013).
- **Stance Profile:**
  - **Anchors High:** `EX-03` (*Independent Method Consilience* & *Exhaustive Systematic Closure*), `EX-04` (*Rigorous Independent Verification*), `EX-07` (*Systematic-Control Obsession*), `EX-17` (*Ironclad 5σ Standard* or *Context-Scaled Threshold*), `EX-18` (*Strict Hidden-Offset / Salted Blinding*), `EX-19` (*Feldman–Cousins Unified Intervals* / *Frequentist Profile Likelihood & CLs*), `EX-20` (*Pure Data-Driven Sidebands*), `EX-21` (*Physics-Explicit Cut-Based*), `EX-22` (*Assume Loose Cable / Hidden Systematic*), `EX-32` (High *Mandatory Two-Team Independent Analysis*).
  - **Anchors Low:** `EX-17` (*Report 2σ–3σ Tensions Early*), `EX-18` (*Unblinded Exploratory Intuition*), `EX-22` (*Treat as New-Physics Lead*).

### 6. The Deep-Underground Rare-Event Hunter
- **Summary:** Battles cosmic rays and radioactive trace atoms beneath mountains to isolate single elusive interactions from neutrinos, neutrinoless double-beta decay, or dark matter.
- **Primary Thinkers & Literature:** R. Davis Jr., A. B. McDonald (SNO), modern XENON/LZ/Super-Kamiokande/GERDA underground low-background tradition.
- **Stance Profile:**

### 7. The Exploratory Instrumentalist
