# Skin 5: Simulcapades — Computational Physics & Scientific Simulation

## 1. Domain Overview & Core Fault Lines

Computational physics and scientific simulation treat the digital computer as a virtual laboratory—bridging analytical theory and physical experiment across length and time scales unreachable by either alone. (*Note: This domain focuses strictly on scientific modeling, numerical computation, and in-silico experimentation, not Nick Bostrom's metaphysical simulation argument.*) Five deep fault lines shape the discipline:

1. **Epistemic Status — "Third Pillar of Science" vs. Automated Deduction:** Does scientific simulation generate genuinely novel, quasi-experimental knowledge alongside theory and lab work, or is it ultimately a numerical calculator constrained by whatever assumptions we typed into its equations? (Humphreys 2004; Winsberg 2010; Parker 2009).
2. **Ab Initio Realism vs. Coarse-Grained & Phenomenological Reach:** Should computational scientists insist on parameter-free quantum or direct-numerical-simulation (DNS) fidelity, or build hierarchical coarse-grained and parameterized models that actually reach macroscopic timescales and emergent complexity? (Car & Parrinello 1985; Moin & Mahesh 1998 vs. Held 2005; Marrink et al. 2007).
3. **Physics-Based Numerical Solvers vs. Scientific Machine Learning (SciML) & Neural Emulators:** Should ML serve strictly as a symmetry-constrained accelerator (neural interatomic potentials, learned subgrid closures), or can end-to-end neural operators and foundation models replace classical PDE time-steppers? (Behler & Parrinello 2007; Raissi et al. 2019; Li et al. 2021; Lam et al. 2023).
4. **Structure-Preserving Numerical Elegance vs. Brute-Force GPU Scaling:** Should simulators prioritize exact discrete conservation laws, symplectic geometry, and high-order convergence, or favor simpler, GPU-friendly kernels that ride exascale hardware throughput? (Hairer, Lubich & Wanner 2006; LeVeque 2002).
5. **Verified Community Software Infrastructure vs. Agile Research Scripts:** Should scientific codes be built as open-source, unit-tested, containerized community platforms subjected to formal Verification, Validation, and Uncertainty Quantification (V&V/UQ), or stay lean, bespoke codes that individual researchers iterate rapidly? (Oberkampf & Roy 2010; Wilson et al. 2014).

## 2. Categories

1. **Epistemology of the Virtual Laboratory** — Third pillar vs. deduction, insight vs. prediction, tuning honesty, multiscale coupling, and extrapolation trust.
2. **Modeling Paradigms & Resolution** — Continuum vs. particle vs. Monte Carlo vs. agent-based, ab initio vs. coarse-graining, turbulence closures, rare events, and quantum many-body methods.
3. **Numerical Methods & Discretization Craft** — FD/FV/FEM/Spectral trade-offs, symplectic & conservative schemes, AMR vs. uniform grids, implicit vs. explicit solvers, and floating-point precision.
4. **Machine Learning, Surrogates & Differentiable Simulation** — MLIPs & neural operators, PINNs debate, differentiable physics, hard vs. soft physical constraints, and foundation models.
5. **Verification, Validation & Uncertainty Quantification (V&V/UQ)** — Manufactured solutions, meaning of validation, UQ depth, bitwise vs. statistical reproducibility, and blind intercomparisons.
6. **HPC Architecture, Software Craft & Culture** — Language stacks, algorithms vs. hardware, flagship codes vs. bespoke scripts, open source, RSE credit, and green HPC.
7. **Linear Algebra, Solvers & Fast Algorithms** — Direct vs. iterative Krylov solvers, algebraic vs. geometric multigrid, FMM vs. FFT, randomized linear algebra, and domain decomposition.
8. **Stochastic, Kinetic & Astrophysical/Plasma Simulation** — Eulerian vs. SPH vs. moving-mesh hydro, PIC vs. continuum Vlasov kinetics, kinetic Monte Carlo, MCMC/HMC sampling, and radiation transport.
9. **Data Assimilation, Digital Twins & Reduced-Order Modeling** — 4D-Var vs. Ensemble Kalman Filters, digital-twin realism vs. hype, POD/Galerkin vs. latent autoencoders, multifidelity UQ, and live experimental coupling.

## 3. Topics (52 Topics)

### Category 1: Epistemology of the Virtual Laboratory

#### `SI-01` — Epistemic Status of Scientific Simulation
- **Category:** Epistemology of the Virtual Laboratory
- **Geometry:** `triangle`
- **Poles:** `Automated Mathematical Deduction` | `Genuine Virtual Experiment` | `Distinct Third Pillar of Science`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Is a computer simulation just fancy numerical arithmetic unpacking what theory already implied, a legitimate experiment performed on a silicon substitute, or a fundamentally new third mode of science?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Automated Mathematical Deduction:* A simulation never touches physical reality; it can only reveal logical consequences of the equations and approximations programmed into it.
  - *Genuine Virtual Experiment:* Exploring nonlinear, chaotic, and multi-physics codes involves surprise, instrumentation bugs, and intervention exactly analogous to laboratory experiments.
  - *Distinct Third Pillar of Science:* Simulation fuses theoretical models, numerical craft, and empirical calibration into an autonomous epistemic pillar beside theory and experiment.
- **Primary citations:** F. Rohrlich (1990), *PSA*; P. Humphreys (2004), *Extending Ourselves*; W. S. Parker (2009), *Synthese*; E. Winsberg (2010), *Science in the Age of Computer Simulation*.

#### `SI-02` — Prediction Fidelity vs. Conceptual Understanding
- **Category:** Epistemology of the Virtual Laboratory
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Minimal Models for Insight` | `Hierarchical Model Ladder` | `High-Fidelity Quantitative Prediction`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** As complex simulations (e.g., Earth-system or galaxy-formation models) become almost as opaque as nature itself, should we strip them down for understanding or push resolution ever higher?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Minimal Models for Insight:* "The purpose of computing is insight, not numbers" (Hamming); a giant black-box simulator that reproduces reality without explaining *why* misses the point of physics.
  - *Hierarchical Model Ladder:* Pair realistic flagship simulations with a ladder of simplified idealized models so every emergent phenomenon can be traced to its core mechanism (Held).
  - *High-Fidelity Quantitative Prediction:* Real systems are irreducibly coupled; oversimplified toy models mislead, whereas high-resolution realism captures critical nonlinear feedbacks accurately.
- **Primary citations:** R. W. Hamming (1962), *Numerical Methods for Scientists and Engineers*; I. M. Held (2005), "The Gap Between Simulation and Understanding in Climate Modeling," *Bull. Am. Meteorol. Soc.*

#### `SI-03` — Subgrid Tuning & Parameterization Honesty
- **Category:** Epistemology of the Virtual Laboratory
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Reject Ad-Hoc Tuning—Resolve Physics` | `Transparent Documented Calibration` | `Pragmatic Tuning for System Skill`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Unresolved subgrid physics (clouds, star formation, turbulence viscosity) requires tunable closure parameters; is tuning necessary craft or scientific fudge-factoring?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Reject Ad-Hoc Tuning—Resolve Physics:* Tuning free closure constants masks structural errors with compensating biases; invest compute in resolving the underlying physics directly.
  - *Transparent Documented Calibration:* Tuning is unavoidable in finite-resolution models, so treat it as an explicit, published, reproducible inverse problem with held-out validation metrics.
  - *Pragmatic Tuning for System Skill:* Forecasting real-world systems requires calibrating effective subgrid closures to observations—judge models by out-of-sample skill, not purism.
- **Primary citations:** F. Hourdin et al. (2017), "The Art and Science of Climate Model Tuning," *Bull. Am. Meteorol. Soc.*; E. Winsberg (2010).

#### `SI-04` — Multiscale Coupling Architecture
- **Category:** Epistemology of the Virtual Laboratory
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Concurrent Handshaking (QM/MM, Coupled)` | `Sequential Parameter Passing` | `Autonomous Single-Scale Effective Models`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** When phenomena span quantum electrons to continuum mechanics, how should simulators bridge the scales?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Concurrent Handshaking (QM/MM, Coupled):* Dynamically couple fine and coarse solvers on the fly where localized reactive or crack-tip physics feeds back into the continuum.
  - *Sequential Parameter Passing:* Run accurate microscale simulations offline to extract equations of state, transport coefficients, or force fields for clean macroscale codes.
  - *Autonomous Single-Scale Effective Models:* Scale separation usually holds; clean renormalization-group effective models avoid the notorious artifact boundaries of hybrid handshaking zones.
- **Primary citations:** A. Warshel & M. Levitt (1976), *J. Mol. Biol.* (QM/MM); W. E & B. Engquist (2003), "The Heterogeneous Multiscale Methods," *Commun. Math. Sci.*

#### `SI-05` — Trusting Simulations in Unobserved Regimes
- **Category:** Epistemology of the Virtual Laboratory
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Never Trust Beyond Validated Data` | `Physics-Bounded Extrapolation with UQ` | `First-Principles Laws Guarantee Reach`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Many premier simulations—planetary cores, century-scale climate, nuclear stockpiles, black-hole mergers—predict regimes where direct experiments are impossible; how far does trust extend?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Never Trust Beyond Validated Data:* Validation only tests the interpolation envelope; extrapolating any numerical model into unmeasured regimes is inherently fragile (Oreskes et al.).
  - *Physics-Bounded Extrapolation with UQ:* Extrapolation is credible when anchored to exact conservation laws, hierarchical sub-component tests, and honest structural-discrepancy uncertainty bounds.
  - *First-Principles Laws Guarantee Reach:* General relativity, Maxwell's equations, and quantum mechanics hold universally—numerically converged solutions of fundamental equations predict unobserved regimes reliably.
- **Primary citations:** N. Oreskes, K. Shrader-Frechette & K. Belitz (1994), "Verification, Validation, and Confirmation of Numerical Models in the Earth Sciences," *Science*; W. L. Oberkampf & C. J. Roy (2010).

### Category 2: Modeling Paradigms & Resolution

#### `SI-06` — Primary Simulation Worldview
- **Category:** Modeling Paradigms & Resolution
- **Geometry:** `quad-compass`
- **Poles:** `Continuum PDE Field Solvers` | `Deterministic Particle & Molecular Dynamics` | `Stochastic Monte Carlo & Lattice` | `Discrete-Event & Agent-Based`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Which computational representation of physical and complex systems feels most natural and authoritative to you?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Continuum PDE Field Solvers:* Fluids, plasmas, elastodynamics, and curved spacetimes are cleanest as smooth partial differential equations discretized on meshes.
  - *Deterministic Particle & Molecular Dynamics:* Integrating Newton's or Hamilton's equations for millions of interacting particles reveals real-time microscopic dynamics directly.
  - *Stochastic Monte Carlo & Lattice:* Path integrals, equilibrium phase transitions, and non-perturbative QCD demand importance sampling over vast configuration spaces.
  - *Discrete-Event & Agent-Based:* Heterogeneous, adaptive, far-from-equilibrium systems emerge naturally from local interaction rules among discrete autonomous entities.
- **Primary citations:** N. Metropolis et al. (1953), *J. Chem. Phys.*; B. J. Alder & T. E. Wainwright (1957), *J. Chem. Phys.*; K. G. Wilson (1974), *Phys. Rev. D*; J. M. Epstein & R. Axtell (1996).

#### `SI-07` — Ab Initio Fidelity vs. Coarse-Grained Reach
- **Category:** Modeling Paradigms & Resolution
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Parameter-Free Ab Initio First` | `Bottom-Up Systematic Coarse-Graining` | `Top-Down Phenomenological Reach`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should atomistic and condensed-matter simulation prioritize quantum-accurate electrons over short picoseconds, or lump atoms into coarse beads to watch milliseconds of self-assembly?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Parameter-Free Ab Initio First:* Empirical force fields break down whenever bonds form, charge transfers, or environments shift; ab initio electronic structure (AIMD/QMC) is the only honest baseline.
  - *Bottom-Up Systematic Coarse-Graining:* Derive reduced potentials systematically via force-matching or relative entropy from ab initio data so fidelity and scale reinforce each other.
  - *Top-Down Phenomenological Reach:* Calibrating coarse beads (like MARTINI) to thermodynamic partitioning lets us actually simulate membranes, viruses, and phase separation at realistic scales.
- **Primary citations:** R. Car & M. Parrinello (1985), *Phys. Rev. Lett.*; S. J. Marrink et al. (2007), *J. Phys. Chem. B*; W. G. Noid (2013), *J. Chem. Phys.*

#### `SI-08` — Turbulence & Nonlinear Cascade Strategy
- **Category:** Modeling Paradigms & Resolution
- **Geometry:** `triangle`
- **Poles:** `Direct Numerical Simulation (DNS)` | `Large-Eddy Simulation (LES)` | `Reynolds-Averaged Closures (RANS)`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Facing Kolmogorov's brutal $Re^{9/4}$ scaling of degrees of freedom in turbulent flows, where do you draw the line between resolving eddies and modeling them?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Direct Numerical Simulation (DNS):* Resolve all the way down to the Kolmogorov dissipation scale with zero turbulence-model bias—even if Reynolds numbers stay modest.
  - *Large-Eddy Simulation (LES):* Explicitly simulate the problem-dependent energy-containing eddies and model only the more universal subgrid inertial-dissipation tail.
  - *Reynolds-Averaged Closures (RANS):* Engineering design sweeps across thousands of geometries demand fast, reliable statistical closures rather than hero runs taking months.
- **Primary citations:** P. Moin & K. Mahesh (1998), "Direct Numerical Simulation: A Tool in Turbulence Research," *Annu. Rev. Fluid Mech.*; S. B. Pope (2000), *Turbulent Flows*.

#### `SI-09` — Rare Events & Conformational Sampling
- **Category:** Modeling Paradigms & Resolution
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Brute-Force Long Unbiased Trajectories` | `Enhanced Biasing (Metadynamics / Umbrella)` | `Path Sampling & Markov State Models`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** When nucleation, protein folding, or reaction barriers take microseconds to seconds while timesteps are femtoseconds, how should simulators conquer rare events?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Brute-Force Long Unbiased Trajectories:* Adding biasing potentials distorts pathways and misses hidden collective variables; specialized ultra-fast hardware running unbiased dynamics tells the unvarnished truth (Anton ethos).
  - *Enhanced Biasing (Metadynamics / Umbrella):* Smart collective variables and history-dependent biases reconstruct free-energy landscapes orders of magnitude faster than brute force.
  - *Path Sampling & Markov State Models:* Adaptive ensembles of short unbiased trajectories stitched into transition networks or TPS capture true kinetics without artificial force bias.
- **Primary citations:** D. E. Shaw et al. (2008), *Commun. ACM* (Anton); A. Laio & M. Parrinello (2002), *PNAS* (Metadynamics); P. G. Bolhuis et al. (2002), *Annu. Rev. Phys. Chem.* (TPS).

#### `SI-10` — Quantum Many-Body Simulation Frontier
- **Category:** Modeling Paradigms & Resolution
- **Geometry:** `triangle`
- **Poles:** `Quantum Monte Carlo & Lattice QFT` | `Tensor Networks (DMRG / PEPS)` | `Analog & Digital Quantum Simulators`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Facing the exponential Hilbert-space wall and fermion sign problem, which computational engine will crack strongly correlated quantum matter?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Quantum Monte Carlo & Lattice QFT:* Stochastic sampling and clever basis/constraint tricks (AFQMC, fixed-node DMC, Lattice QCD) deliver unmatched 3D precision across physics.
  - *Tensor Networks (DMRG / PEPS):* Area-law entanglement structure lets matrix-product states and projected entangled-pair states defeat the sign problem deterministically.
  - *Analog & Digital Quantum Simulators:* As Feynman foresaw, classical bits hit an exponential wall for real-time quantum dynamics; programmable neutral atoms, ions, and qubits are the ultimate simulator.
- **Primary citations:** R. P. Feynman (1982), "Simulating Physics with Computers," *Int. J. Theor. Phys.*; S. R. White (1992), *Phys. Rev. Lett.* (DMRG); M. Troyer & U.-J. Wiese (2005), *Phys. Rev. Lett.* (sign problem).

#### `SI-11` — Single Hero Run vs. Large Statistical Ensembles
- **Category:** Modeling Paradigms & Resolution
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `One Ultra-High-Resolution Hero Run` | `Resolution-Ensemble Compromise` | `Massive Lower-Res Probabilistic Ensembles`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Given a fixed supercomputer allocation for chaotic dynamics (weather, astrophysics, plasma), should you spend it on one breathtaking ultra-fine run or 500 coarser ensemble members?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *One Ultra-High-Resolution Hero Run:* Crossing a critical physical resolution threshold (resolving convection, shocks, or magneto-rotational instability) changes the qualitative physics completely.
  - *Resolution-Ensemble Compromise:* Run a small ensemble at high enough resolution to separate physical regime shifts from chaotic initial-condition spread.
  - *Massive Lower-Res Probabilistic Ensembles:* In chaotic systems, one deterministic trajectory is statistically meaningless; large ensembles with stochastic physics quantify risk and tail events honestly.
- **Primary citations:** E. N. Lorenz (1963), *J. Atmos. Sci.*; T. N. Palmer (2019), "Stochastic Weather and Climate Models," *Nat. Rev. Phys.*

### Category 3: Numerical Methods & Discretization Craft

#### `SI-12` — Spatial Discretization Preference
- **Category:** Numerical Methods & Discretization Craft
- **Geometry:** `diamond`
- **Poles:** `Finite Differences (FD)` | `Conservative Finite Volumes (FV)` | `Finite Elements & Discontinuous Galerkin` | `Spectral & Meshfree Particle Methods`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** When discretizing continuum physics onto a computer, which numerical family is your gold standard?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Finite Differences (FD):* Simple, fast, cache-friendly, and trivially extensible to high-order stencils on structured grids.
  - *Conservative Finite Volumes (FV):* Guarantees exact discrete conservation of mass, momentum, and energy across shocks and discontinuities via flux-balanced cells.
  - *Finite Elements & Discontinuous Galerkin:* Handles intricate engineering geometries with rigorous weak-form error bounds and arbitrary polynomial order ($hp$-adaptivity).
  - *Spectral & Meshfree Particle Methods:* Spectral methods achieve exponential convergence on smooth domains, while SPH/meshfree methods track violent free surfaces and fragmentation effortlessly.
- **Primary citations:** R. J. LeVeque (2002), *Finite Volume Methods for Hyperbolic Problems*; L. N. Trefethen (2000), *Spectral Methods in MATLAB*; J. S. Hesthaven & T. Warburton (2008), *Nodal Discontinuous Galerkin Methods*.

#### `SI-13` — Structure-Preserving Geometric Integration
- **Category:** Numerical Methods & Discretization Craft
- **Geometry:** `monopole-slider`
- **Poles:** `Insistence on Symplectic & Exact-Conservation Integrators`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should numerical algorithms strictly preserve the continuous system's geometric invariants (symplectic phase-space volume, $\nabla\cdot\mathbf{B}=0$, exact energy/momentum) rather than merely shrinking truncation error?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Insistence on Symplectic & Exact-Conservation Integrators:* Over millions of orbits or timesteps, non-symplectic solvers drift catastrophically; preserving discrete geometry keeps qualitative long-term physics faithful.
- **Primary citations:** E. Hairer, C. Lubich & G. Wanner (2006), *Geometric Numerical Integration*; C. R. Evans & J. F. Hawley (1988), *ApJ* (constrained transport).

#### `SI-14` — Adaptive Mesh Refinement (AMR) vs. Uniform Grids
- **Category:** Numerical Methods & Discretization Craft
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Clean Uniform Structured Grids` | `Static Nested / Unstructured Meshes` | `Dynamic Adaptive Mesh Refinement`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should simulations concentrate resolution dynamically where gradients explode, or avoid coarse-fine reflection artifacts and load-imbalance headaches with uniform grids?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Clean Uniform Structured Grids:* Uniform grids eliminate spurious wave reflections at refinement boundaries, vectorize effortlessly on GPUs, and have crystal-clear numerical dissipation.
  - *Static Nested / Unstructured Meshes:* Pre-adapted static grids capture known geometric bottlenecks cleanly without dynamic regridding overhead or shifting truncation error.
  - *Dynamic Adaptive Mesh Refinement:* Multi-decade spatial dynamic range (star formation, shock fronts, black-hole inspiral) is computationally impossible without dynamic AMR.
- **Primary citations:** M. J. Berger & J. Oliger (1984), *J. Comput. Phys.*; M. J. Berger & P. Colella (1989), *J. Comput. Phys.*

#### `SI-15` — Explicit Scalability vs. Implicit Stiff Solvers
- **Category:** Numerical Methods & Discretization Craft
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Explicit Local Time-Stepping` | `IMEX Operator Splitting` | `Fully Implicit Newton–Krylov`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** When fast waves or stiff chemical/diffusive timescales restrict the CFL condition, do you take millions of tiny cheap explicit steps or invert global coupled systems implicitly?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Explicit Local Time-Stepping:* Simple stencil updates have near-ideal GPU arithmetic intensity and parallel scalability with zero fragile nonlinear-solver stalls.
  - *IMEX Operator Splitting:* Treat stiff local diffusion or reactions implicitly while stepping advection explicitly—getting the best of both stability and speed.
  - *Fully Implicit Newton–Krylov:* Coupled multi-physics (fusion MHD, mantle convection, stiff combustion) require preconditioned fully implicit solvers to advance on the physical timescale of interest.
- **Primary citations:** U. M. Ascher, S. J. Ruuth & R. J. Spiteri (1997), *Appl. Numer. Math.* (IMEX); D. A. Knoll & D. E. Keyes (2004), *J. Comput. Phys.* (JFNK).

#### `SI-16` — Floating-Point Precision Discipline
- **Category:** Numerical Methods & Discretization Craft
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Strict FP64 (or Higher) Everywhere` | `Mixed Precision with FP64 Accumulation` | `Embrace Low-Precision Tensor Cores`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Modern AI-driven GPUs offer massive speedups in FP16/BF16/FP32 at the expense of FP64 throughput; how aggressively should scientific codes lower precision?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Strict FP64 (or Higher) Everywhere:* Round-off accumulation, ill-conditioned linear solves, and catastrophic cancellation silently corrupt physics; never gamble scientific accuracy for cheap FLOPs.
  - *Mixed Precision with FP64 Accumulation:* Run iterative refinement, preconditioners, and pairwise forces in lower precision while keeping global sums and outer residuals strictly FP64.
  - *Embrace Low-Precision Tensor Cores:* Most simulations have discretization or model error orders of magnitude larger than $10^{-7}$; low-precision compute buys vastly higher resolution or bigger ensembles.
- **Primary citations:** N. J. Higham (2002), *Accuracy and Stability of Numerical Algorithms*; A. Abdelfattah et al. (2021), "A Survey of Numerical Linear Algebra Methods Utilizing Mixed-Precision Arithmetic," *IJHPCA*.

---

### Category 4: Machine Learning, Surrogates & Differentiable Simulation

#### `SI-17` — ML Surrogates vs. Classical Physics Solvers
- **Category:** Machine Learning, Surrogates & Differentiable Simulation
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Pure First-Principles Solvers Only` | `Hybrid ML-Accelerated Physics` | `End-to-End Neural Operators & Emulators`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** What is the rightful place of deep learning in computational physics—strictly banned from production physics, embedded inside classical solvers, or replacing classical PDE steppers outright?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Pure First-Principles Solvers Only:* Neural surrogates fail silently outside training distributions and lack convergence theorems; keep production physics purely equation-based.
  - *Hybrid ML-Accelerated Physics:* Combine exact continuum/molecular integrators with ML interatomic potentials (MLIPs) or learned subgrid closures to gain $10^4\times$ speedups with physical guardrails.
  - *End-to-End Neural Operators & Emulators:* Learned weather models (GraphCast) and Fourier neural operators already match or beat classical solvers at a fraction of the energy and wall-clock time.
- **Primary citations:** J. Behler & M. Parrinello (2007), *Phys. Rev. Lett.*; Z. Li et al. (2021), *ICLR* (Fourier Neural Operator); R. Lam et al. (2023), "Learning Skillful Medium-Range Global Weather Forecasting," *Science*.

#### `SI-18` — Physics-Informed Neural Networks (PINNs): Promise or Hype?
- **Category:** Machine Learning, Surrogates & Differentiable Simulation
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Overhyped vs. Classical FEM/FV` | `Strong for Inverse & Noisy Data Assimilation` | `Transformative for High-Dimensional PDEs`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Embedding PDE residuals into neural-network loss functions (PINNs) exploded across computational literature—do they outperform classical solvers or solve a different problem?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Overhyped vs. Classical FEM/FV:* On forward PDE benchmarks, classical FEM/FV/spectral methods beat PINNs by orders of magnitude in accuracy, speed, and reliability.
  - *Strong for Inverse & Noisy Data Assimilation:* PINNs shine not as forward PDE replacements, but when fusing sparse, noisy experimental measurements with partial physical equations to infer hidden parameters.
  - *Transformative for High-Dimensional PDEs:* Mesh-free neural representations break the curse of dimensionality in high-dimensional Fokker–Planck, Hamilton–Jacobi, and Schrödinger equations.
- **Primary citations:** M. Raissi, P. Perdikaris & G. E. Karniadakis (2019), *J. Comput. Phys.*; T. G. Grossmann et al. (2024), *IMA J. Appl. Math.* (PINN vs. FEM systematic comparison).

#### `SI-19` — Differentiable Physics & Automatic Differentiation
- **Category:** Machine Learning, Surrogates & Differentiable Simulation
- **Geometry:** `monopole-slider`
- **Poles:** `Priority of End-to-End Differentiable Simulators`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should the next generation of physics simulators be written natively in AD-capable frameworks (JAX, PyTorch, Enzyme, Zygote) so exact gradients flow through entire trajectories?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Priority of End-to-End Differentiable Simulators:* Reverse-mode adjoints through a simulator turn inverse design, sensitivity analysis, optimal control, and ML-closure training from intractable black-box searches into fast gradient descent.
- **Primary citations:** A. Griewank & A. Walther (2008), *Evaluating Derivatives*; S. S. Schoenholz & E. D. Cubuk (2020), *NeurIPS* (JAX MD); D. Kochkov et al. (2021), *PNAS*.

#### `SI-20` — Hard Symmetry Constraints vs. Soft Scale-Driven Learning in SciML
- **Category:** Machine Learning, Surrogates & Differentiable Simulation
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Hard Equivariance & Exact Conservation` | `Pragmatic Inductive Biases` | `Bitter-Lesson Scale Learns Symmetries Softly`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should scientific neural architectures hard-code $E(3)$ equivariance, gauge invariance, and exact conservation laws, or let large, simple unconstrained architectures learn them from data?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Hard Equivariance & Exact Conservation:* Baking rotational/translational symmetry and energy-conservation into network layers dramatically boosts data efficiency and physical stability (NequIP/MACE).
  - *Pragmatic Inductive Biases:* Enforce cheap, high-leverage physical constraints while avoiding overly rigid specialized layers that bottleneck GPU throughput.
  - *Bitter-Lesson Scale Learns Symmetries Softly:* History in AI shows general scalable architectures with data augmentation and huge compute eventually outstrip hand-crafted inductive constraints.
- **Primary citations:** S. Batzner et al. (2022), *Nat. Commun.* (NequIP); R. Sutton (2019), "The Bitter Lesson" (applied to SciML debates on equivariant vs. unconstrained transformers).

#### `SI-21` — AlphaFold & Scientific Foundation Models
- **Category:** Machine Learning, Surrogates & Differentiable Simulation
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Black-Box Pattern Matcher Missing Physics` | `Powerful Catalyst Needing Physical Integration` | `New Blueprint for Computational Science`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Does AlphaFold's triumph over physics-based molecular-dynamics protein folding herald the future of all simulation science, or represent a special data-rich anomaly?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Black-Box Pattern Matcher Missing Physics:* AlphaFold memorizes evolutionary co-variation to predict static crystal structures without explaining folding pathways, dynamics, or novel non-biological chemistry.
  - *Powerful Catalyst Needing Physical Integration:* Foundation models excel at jumping straight to good initial hypotheses, which physics-based simulations then refine for dynamics, free energies, and out-of-distribution states.
  - *New Blueprint for Computational Science:* Pretraining multimodal foundation models across massive simulated and experimental datasets will unify materials, weather, plasma, and molecular design.
- **Primary citations:** J. Jumper et al. (2021), "Highly Accurate Protein Structure Prediction with AlphaFold," *Nature*; D. Baker & D. Hassabis/J. Jumper (2024), Nobel Prize in Chemistry paradigm.

### Category 5: Verification, Validation & Uncertainty Quantification (V&V/UQ)

#### `SI-22` — Formal Code Verification Discipline
- **Category:** Verification, Validation & Uncertainty Quantification (V&V/UQ)
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Physical Sanity Checks Suffice` | `Unit Tests & Mesh Convergence` | `Method of Manufactured Solutions (MMS)`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** How rigorously must you prove your code is "solving the equations right" before running production science?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Physical Sanity Checks Suffice:* Testing known analytical benchmarks (e.g., Sod shock tube, harmonic oscillator) and conservation diagnostics catches practical bugs without bureaucracy.
  - *Unit Tests & Mesh Convergence:* Automated CI unit tests plus systematic grid-refinement convergence studies ensure numerical errors shrink at the advertised order.
  - *Method of Manufactured Solutions (MMS):* Only MMS exercises every nonlinear coupled term and boundary condition simultaneously to verify exact theoretical order of accuracy down to machine round-off.
- **Primary citations:** P. J. Roache (1998), *Verification and Validation in Computational Science and Engineering*; W. L. Oberkampf & C. J. Roy (2010), *Verification and Validation in Scientific Computing*.

#### `SI-23` — Validation Philosophy: What Does "Validated" Mean?
- **Category:** Verification, Validation & Uncertainty Quantification (V&V/UQ)
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Models Are Never Validated—Only Falsifiable` | `Fit-for-Purpose Over Declared Domain` | `Quantitative Predictive Certification`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** When a simulation matches a handful of laboratory measurements, can we ever honestly call the model "validated"?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Models Are Never Validated—Only Falsifiable:* Calling an open natural-system model "validated" creates false certainty; matching past data never proves uniqueness or future correctness (Oreskes et al.).
  - *Fit-for-Purpose Over Declared Domain:* Validation is never binary or global—it is an ongoing statement that a model's bias is acceptable for a specific decision within a tested regime.
  - *Quantitative Predictive Certification:* Through hierarchical validation experiments, error metrics, and statistical tolerance intervals, engineering-grade simulations can be quantitatively certified for safety-critical use.
- **Primary citations:** N. Oreskes, K. Shrader-Frechette & K. Belitz (1994), *Science*; W. L. Oberkampf & C. J. Roy (2010).

#### `SI-24` — Uncertainty Quantification (UQ) Rigor
- **Category:** Verification, Validation & Uncertainty Quantification (V&V/UQ)
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Deterministic Best-Estimate Point Runs` | `Parametric Sensitivity Sweeps` | `Full Bayesian & Model-Discrepancy UQ`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should every published computational prediction carry full propagated error bars accounting for numerical discretization, parameter uncertainty, and structural model discrepancy?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Deterministic Best-Estimate Point Runs:* When a single frontier simulation takes millions of core-hours, spending 90% of the budget on UQ ensembles leaves you simulating the wrong resolution.
  - *Parametric Sensitivity Sweeps:* Perturbing key inputs and numerical resolutions brackets dominant sensitivities cleanly without over-formalizing unknown priors.
  - *Full Bayesian & Model-Discrepancy UQ:* A simulation output without error bars is not a scientific measurement; Kennedy–O'Hagan calibration, polynomial chaos, and structural discrepancy modeling are mandatory.
- **Primary citations:** M. C. Kennedy & A. O'Hagan (2001), "Bayesian Calibration of Computer Models," *JRSS-B*; R. C. Smith (2013), *Uncertainty Quantification: Theory, Implementation, and Applications*.

#### `SI-25` — Bitwise Determinism vs. Statistical Reproducibility
- **Category:** Verification, Validation & Uncertainty Quantification (V&V/UQ)
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Statistical Equivalence Is Enough` | `Seeded Same-Stack Reproducibility` | `Strict Bitwise & Containerized Replay`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Because parallel floating-point reductions are non-associative across thread counts and GPUs, should scientific simulations demand bit-for-bit identical replay or only statistically equivalent distributions?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Statistical Equivalence Is Enough:* Any chaotic physical simulation diverges exponentially from $10^{-16}$ perturbations; demanding bitwise identity across architectures confuses numerical artifacts with physical truth.
  - *Seeded Same-Stack Reproducibility:* Preserve exact random seeds, input decks, container images, and software hashes so colleagues can reproduce published figures reliably.
  - *Strict Bitwise & Containerized Replay:* Bit-for-bit determinism is priceless for catching regression bugs, compiler miscompilations, and race conditions during code development and audit.
- **Primary citations:** D. L. Donoho (2010), "An Invitation to Reproducible Computational Research," *Biostatistics*; V. Stodden et al. (2016), "Enhancing Reproducibility for Computational Methods," *Science*.

#### `SI-26` — Blind Multi-Code Intercomparisons
- **Category:** Verification, Validation & Uncertainty Quantification (V&V/UQ)
- **Geometry:** `monopole-slider`
- **Poles:** `Mandatory Multi-Code Benchmark Intercomparisons`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** How crucial are community-wide intercomparison projects (like CMIP in climate, CASP in protein folding, or the Santa Barbara Cluster Comparison in astrophysics) where independent codes run identical test problems?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Mandatory Multi-Code Benchmark Intercomparisons:* Single-code studies hide method-specific artifacts (e.g., SPH vs. AMR entropy cores); pitting independent codes against blind benchmarks exposes real physical consensus.
- **Primary citations:** C. S. Frenk et al. (1999), "The Santa Barbara Cluster Comparison Project," *ApJ*; CMIP (Coupled Model Intercomparison Project) & CASP frameworks.

---

### Category 6: HPC Architecture, Software Craft & Culture

#### `SI-27` — Scientific Computing Language Stack
- **Category:** HPC Architecture, Software Craft & Culture
- **Geometry:** `quad-compass`
- **Poles:** `Fortran & C/C++ Bare Metal` | `Python Orchestration + Compiled Kernels` | `Julia Unified Language` | `Rust & Modern Systems Safety`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Which programming ecosystem best balances raw supercomputer performance, mathematical expressiveness, and long-term maintainability?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Fortran & C/C++ Bare Metal:* Decades of battle-tested compilers, MPI/CUDA/Kokkos ecosystems, and predictable zero-overhead performance make Fortran and C++ irreplaceable at exascale.
  - *Python Orchestration + Compiled Kernels:* Write expressive scientific workflows in Python/NumPy/JAX while delegating hot loops to C++/CUDA/XLA—maximum productivity with near-native speed.
  - *Julia Unified Language:* Multiple dispatch and LLVM JIT solve the "two-language problem" cleanly, letting scientists write readable math that runs at C speed natively.
  - *Rust & Modern Systems Safety:* Memory safety, fearless concurrency, and modern package tooling eliminate whole classes of subtle segfaults and data races plaguing legacy HPC codes.
- **Primary citations:** J. Bezanson et al. (2017), "Julia: A Fresh Approach to Numerical Computing," *SIAM Rev.*; C. R. Harris et al. (2020), *Nature* (NumPy); practitioner HPC stack debates.

#### `SI-28` — Algorithmic Innovation vs. Hardware/GPU Scaling
- **Category:** HPC Architecture, Software Craft & Culture
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Algorithms Beat Hardware Every Time` | `Algorithm–Hardware Co-Design` | `Ride the Exascale GPU Wave`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Where does bigger real-world speedup come from—inventing $O(N \log N)$ or $O(N)$ algorithms (FFT, Fast Multipole, multigrid), or refactoring legacy codes aggressively for GPUs and accelerators?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Algorithms Beat Hardware Every Time:* Replacing an $O(N^2)$ loop with an $O(N)$ Fast Multipole or multigrid solver delivers speedups that ten generations of Moore's Law cannot match.
  - *Algorithm–Hardware Co-Design:* Modern bottlenecks are memory bandwidth and communication latency; winning methods are mathematically optimal *and* cache/GPU-aware from day one.
  - *Ride the Exascale GPU Wave:* Simple, regular, massively parallel algorithms that saturate modern GPU/TPU silicon frequently outperform complex asymptotically faster schemes in wall-clock time.
- **Primary citations:** L. Greengard & V. Rokhlin (1987), *J. Comput. Phys.* (FMM); J. L. Gustafson (1988), *Commun. ACM*; DOE Exascale Computing Project co-design reports.

#### `SI-29` — Flagship Community Codes vs. Bespoke Research Scripts
- **Category:** HPC Architecture, Software Craft & Culture
- **Geometry:** `xy-switchbox`
- **Poles:** `X: Community Flagship Code (LAMMPS/OpenFOAM/VASP) ↔ Bespoke From-Scratch Code` | `Y: Production Software Engineering ↔ Rapid Exploratory Prototyping`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should computational physicists build inside shared multi-institution flagship platforms with strict engineering hygiene, or write nimble custom codes tailored to one research idea?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Community Flagship Code (X-Low):* Leveraging battle-tested solvers, parallel I/O, and shared validation suites avoids reinventing buggy wheels and multiplies community impact.
  - *Bespoke From-Scratch Code (X-High):* Writing a clean 500-line custom solver gives total transparency, freedom to try radical numerical ideas, and zero legacy bloat.
  - *Production Software Engineering (Y-Low):* Version control, CI, modular APIs, and code review prevent silent scientific retractions and make tools outlive a single PhD thesis.
  - *Rapid Exploratory Prototyping (Y-High):* Over-engineering speculative research code before knowing whether the physics idea works kills scientific agility.
- **Primary citations:** G. Wilson et al. (2014), "Best Practices for Scientific Computing," *PLoS Biol.*; S. Plimpton (1995), *J. Comput. Phys.* (LAMMPS); practitioner comp-phys culture.

#### `SI-30` — Open-Source Scientific Software Mandate
- **Category:** HPC Architecture, Software Craft & Culture
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Proprietary / On-Request Binaries OK` | `Open-Source Upon Paper Publication` | `Public Open-Development from Day One`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should every computational paper be required to release its complete source code under an open-source license, or are commercial/closed codes (like VASP or Gaussian) acceptable in peer-reviewed science?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Proprietary / On-Request Binaries OK:* Licensing revenue funds dedicated professional maintenance, bug fixes, and user support that unpaid academic open-source projects often struggle to sustain.
  - *Open-Source Upon Paper Publication:* Keep active work private until submission to protect students from getting scooped, then publish clean reproducible open-source code with the paper.
  - *Public Open-Development from Day One:* Closed scientific code is an unverifiable black box; public repositories, open issues, and permissive licenses are non-negotiable for scientific integrity.
- **Primary citations:** D. C. Ince, L. Hatton & J. Graham-Cumming (2012), "The Case for Open Computer Programs," *Nature*; N. Barnes (2010), "Publish Your Computer Code: It Is Good Enough," *Nature*.

#### `SI-31` — Research Software Engineers (RSEs) & Software Credit
- **Category:** HPC Architecture, Software Craft & Culture
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Every Physicist Must Code Everything` | `Embed Career RSEs Inside Research Groups` | `Software as First-Class Academic Output`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** How should academia value and staff the complex software engineering behind modern simulations?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Every Physicist Must Code Everything:* Handing implementation off to separate software staff disconnects algorithms from physical intuition.
  - *Embed Career RSEs Inside Research Groups:* Pairing domain physicists with professional Research Software Engineers yields faster, cleaner, bug-free science without burning out grad students.
  - *Software as First-Class Academic Output:* Peer-reviewed software papers (JOSS), software citations, and permanent RSE faculty tracks should carry full tenure and funding prestige.
- **Primary citations:** D. S. Katz et al. (2016), "Software Citation Principles," *PeerJ Comput. Sci.*; A. M. Smith et al. (2018), *Journal of Open Source Software (JOSS)*.

#### `SI-32` — Visual Diagnostics vs. Pure Quantitative Metrics
- **Category:** HPC Architecture, Software Craft & Culture
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Quantitative Norms & Residuals Only` | `Metrics Anchored by Visual Sanity Checks` | `Rich Field Visualization Catches Bugs First`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** How much do you rely on 2D/3D movies, streamlines, and rendered field slices versus cold error norms and summary statistics to inspect a simulation?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Quantitative Norms & Residuals Only:* Pretty renderings seduce the eye into seeing patterns that aren't there ("viewgraph physics"); trust rigorous error norms and spectra.
  - *Metrics Anchored by Visual Sanity Checks:* Always look at raw slices alongside convergence norms so subtle boundary glitches or grid imprinting never hide inside averages.
  - *Rich Field Visualization Catches Bugs First:* The human visual cortex spots unphysical reflections, checkerboard modes, and symmetry breaks in a movie in two seconds flat (practitioner folklore).
- **Primary citations:** Practitioner computational-physics and CFD folklore ("never trust a summary number until you've plotted the raw field").

#### `SI-33` — Energy & Carbon Ethics of Exascale Computing
- **Category:** HPC Architecture, Software Craft & Culture
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Scientific Breakthrough Justifies Compute` | `Pragmatic FLOPs-per-Watt Efficiency` | `Strict Compute Carbon Budgeting`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Mega-simulations and large SciML training runs consume megawatt-hours of electricity; how strongly should carbon footprint constrain simulation choices?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Scientific Breakthrough Justifies Compute:* Scientific supercomputing is a tiny fraction of global energy use and directly enables climate, fusion, and clean-materials breakthroughs.
  - *Pragmatic FLOPs-per-Watt Efficiency:* Profile codes, kill wasted idle runs, and prefer efficient algorithms—good performance engineering naturally cuts energy waste too.
  - *Strict Compute Carbon Budgeting:* Report estimated $\text{CO}_2\text{e}$ alongside papers, avoid redundant hyperparameter sweeps, and justify every exascale allocation ethically.
- **Primary citations:** S. Portegies Zwart (2020), "The Ecological Impact of High-Performance Computing in Astrophysics," *Nat. Astron.*; L. Lannelongue, J. Grealey & M. Inouye (2021), *Adv. Sci.*

#### `SI-34` — Aesthetic Ideal of Computational Craft
- **Category:** HPC Architecture, Software Craft & Culture
- **Geometry:** `diamond`
- **Poles:** `Exact Conservation & Clean Convergence` | `Breathtaking Emergent Realism` | `Blazing Exascale Throughput` | `Minimal Toy Code Capturing Essence`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** What makes a computational physics paper or codebase genuinely awe-inspiring?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Exact Conservation & Clean Convergence:* Machine-precision invariant preservation and textbook fourth-order convergence curves.
  - *Breathtaking Emergent Realism:* Watching galaxies collide, turbulence cascade, or membranes fuse spontaneously from simple microscopic laws.
  - *Blazing Exascale Throughput:* Sustaining peak roofline efficiency across tens of thousands of GPUs with linear weak scaling.
  - *Minimal Toy Code Capturing Essence:* A crystal-clear 100-line script that lays bare a profound physical phase transition or instability.
- **Primary citations:** R. W. Hamming (1962); E. Hairer et al. (2006); practitioner computational-physics aesthetics.

---

### Category 7: Linear Algebra, Solvers & Fast Algorithms

#### `SI-35` — Direct Sparse Factorizations vs. Iterative Krylov Solvers
- **Category:** Linear Algebra, Solvers & Fast Algorithms
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Bulletproof Direct Sparse LU/Cholesky` | `Preconditioned Hybrid Solvers` | `Matrix-Free Iterative Krylov Methods`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** When solving giant sparse linear systems $Ax=b$, should you rely on rock-solid direct sparse factorizations (MUMPS/SuperLU) or scale to billions of unknowns with preconditioned CG/GMRES?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Bulletproof Direct Sparse LU/Cholesky:* Direct solvers never stall on ill-conditioned multiphysics matrices and amortize cleanly across hundreds of right-hand sides.
  - *Preconditioned Hybrid Solvers:* Use sparse direct factorizations on local subdomains or coarse grids to build unstoppable preconditioners for outer Krylov iterations.
  - *Matrix-Free Iterative Krylov Methods:* In 3D exascale problems, storing a factored matrix blows memory instantly—only matrix-free operator evaluations scale to $10^{10}$ degrees of freedom.
- **Primary citations:** Y. Saad (2003), *Iterative Methods for Sparse Linear Systems* (2nd ed.); T. A. Davis (2006), *Direct Methods for Sparse Linear Systems*.

#### `SI-36` — Geometric Multigrid vs. Algebraic Multigrid (AMG)
- **Category:** Linear Algebra, Solvers & Fast Algorithms
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Hand-Crafted Geometric Multigrid (GMG)` | `Semi-Algebraic Hybrid Coarsening` | `Black-Box Algebraic Multigrid (AMG)`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Achieving textbook $O(N)$ elliptic solves requires multigrid hierarchies—should you exploit the physical mesh explicitly or construct coarse levels purely from matrix entries?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Hand-Crafted Geometric Multigrid (GMG):* Matrix-free geometric hierarchies hit staggering memory-bandwidth speedups and minimal setup cost on structured or nested domains (Brandt).
  - *Semi-Algebraic Hybrid Coarsening:* Combine geometric $p$- or $h$-coarsening on fine levels with algebraic multigrid at the unstructured coarse bottom.
  - *Black-Box Algebraic Multigrid (AMG):* Real engineering geometries have brutal anisotropy, jumps in conductivity, and unstructured CAD meshes that only AMG coarsens reliably without manual intervention.
- **Primary citations:** A. Brandt (1977), *Math. Comput.*; U. Trottenberg, C. W. Oosterlee & A. Schüller (2001), *Multigrid*; J. Xu & L. Zikatanov (2017), *Acta Numer.*

#### `SI-37` — Fast Multipole & Hierarchical $\mathcal{H}$-Matrices vs. FFT / Particle-Mesh
- **Category:** Linear Algebra, Solvers & Fast Algorithms
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `FFT & Particle-Mesh Ewald (PME)` | `Tree-Code & Hybrid Particle-Mesh` | `Fast Multipole (FMM) & ℋ-Matrices`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** For long-range $1/r$ Coulomb, gravitational, and Stokes interactions, which fast-summation paradigm wins?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *FFT & Particle-Mesh Ewald (PME):* Periodic molecular dynamics and spectral fluid boxes run blazingly fast with mature, GPU-tuned 3D FFTs—until global all-to-all MPI transposes choke scaling.
  - *Tree-Code & Hybrid Particle-Mesh:* Barnes–Hut trees and TreePM combine rapid periodic mesh grids at large scales with adaptive local multipoles inside dense clusters.
  - *Fast Multipole (FMM) & ℋ-Matrices:* True $O(N)$ complexity, natural handling of non-periodic clustered distributions, and low-communication local tree traversals outscale FFTs at extreme core counts.
- **Primary citations:** L. Greengard & V. Rokhlin (1987), *J. Comput. Phys.*; J. Barnes & P. Hut (1986), *Nature*; T. Darden, D. York & L. Pedersen (1993), *J. Chem. Phys.* (PME); W. Hackbusch (2015), *Hierarchical Matrices*.

#### `SI-38` — Randomized Numerical Linear Algebra vs. Classical Deterministic Decompositions
- **Category:** Linear Algebra, Solvers & Fast Algorithms
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Classical Deterministic SVD / QR Only` | `Randomized Sketch + Deterministic Check` | `Randomized Linear Algebra First`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should scientific computing replace classic deterministic decompositions with randomized sketching (randomized SVD, Hutchinson trace estimators, stochastic Lanczos) that slash memory traffic?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Classical Deterministic SVD / QR Only:* Deterministic Golub–Kahan/Householder algorithms give rock-solid backward stability without any probabilistic failure tail.
  - *Randomized Sketch + Deterministic Check:* Use random projections to find the dominant low-rank subspace in two passes, then verify residuals deterministically.
  - *Randomized Linear Algebra First:* With provable exponential failure bounds ($<10^{-16}$), randomized algorithms make massive matrix factorizations, kernel solves, and UQ operators dramatically faster and more cache-friendly.
- **Primary citations:** N. Halko, P.-G. Martinsson & J. A. Tropp (2011), "Finding Structure with Randomness," *SIAM Rev.*; P.-G. Martinsson & J. A. Tropp (2020), *Acta Numer.*

#### `SI-39` — Synchronous Parallelism vs. Communication-Avoiding & Asynchronous Solvers
- **Category:** Linear Algebra, Solvers & Fast Algorithms
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Clean Synchronous Bulk-Parallel MPI` | `Overlap Compute & Non-Blocking Comms` | `Task-Based & Asynchronous Execution`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** As global synchronization barriers increasingly dominate exascale runtime, should codes remain cleanly synchronous or adopt asynchronous task graphs and relaxed synchronization?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Clean Synchronous Bulk-Parallel MPI:* Bulk-synchronous halo exchanges stay easy to debug, verify, and keep bit-reproducible across thousands of ranks.
  - *Overlap Compute & Non-Blocking Comms:* Hide MPI latency behind interior stencil work using non-blocking communications while keeping mathematical equivalence intact.
  - *Task-Based & Asynchronous Execution:* Dynamic task runtimes (Charm++, Legion, HPX) and communication-avoiding Krylov solvers break global barrier walls on heterogeneous exascale clusters.
- **Primary citations:** J. Demmel et al. (2012), "Communication-Optimal Parallel and Sequential QR and LU Factorizations," *SIAM J. Sci. Comput.*; L. V. Kale & S. Krishnan (1993) (Charm++).

#### `SI-40` — Automatic Code Generation & DSLs vs. Hand-Tuned Kernels
- **Category:** Linear Algebra, Solvers & Fast Algorithms
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Hand-Tuned Architecture-Specific Kernels` | `Performance-Portable Abstractions (Kokkos/SYCL)` | `High-Level Symbolic DSLs (Firedrake/FEniCS)`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Facing rapid churn across NVIDIA, AMD, and Intel GPU architectures, how should computational scientists maintain performance without rewriting physics every five years?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Hand-Tuned Architecture-Specific Kernels:* Squeezing the final 2× out of shared memory, warp shuffles, and tensor cores still demands bespoke CUDA/HIP craftsmanship.
  - *Performance-Portable Abstractions (Kokkos/SYCL):* Write one clean C++ parallel loop abstraction that compiles cleanly across CPUs and every vendor's GPU without lock-in.
  - *High-Level Symbolic DSLs (Firedrake/FEniCS):* Express PDEs directly in high-level mathematical weak forms and let compiler pipelines auto-generate optimal vectorized C/GPU kernels automatically.
- **Primary citations:** H. C. Edwards, C. R. Trott & D. Sunderland (2014), *J. Parallel Distrib. Comput.* (Kokkos); M. S. Alnæs et al. (2015), *Arch. Numer. Softw.* (FEniCS); F. Rathgeber et al. (2016) (Firedrake).

---

### Category 8: Stochastic, Kinetic & Astrophysical/Plasma Simulation

#### `SI-41` — Hydrodynamics Paradigm: Eulerian Grid vs. Lagrangian SPH vs. Moving Mesh
- **Category:** Stochastic, Kinetic & Astrophysical/Plasma Simulation
- **Geometry:** `triangle`
- **Poles:** `Eulerian Fixed/AMR Grids` | `Lagrangian Smoothed Particle Hydrodynamics` | `Arbitrary Lagrangian–Eulerian Moving Voronoi Mesh`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** In astrophysics, fluid dynamics, and violent free-surface flows, which hydrodynamics formulation handles shocks, shear instabilities, and Galilean invariance best?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Eulerian Fixed/AMR Grids:* Godunov finite-volume grids capture Kelvin–Helmholtz shear instabilities and strong shocks sharply without particle noise.
  - *Lagrangian Smoothed Particle Hydrodynamics:* Particles automatically follow collapsing gas, conserve angular momentum cleanly, and respect exact Galilean invariance with zero advection error.
  - *Arbitrary Lagrangian–Eulerian Moving Voronoi Mesh:* Moving-mesh codes (AREPO) combine Lagrangian flow tracking with crisp Riemann-solver shock accuracy—getting the best of both worlds.
- **Primary citations:** L. B. Lucy (1977) & R. A. Gingold & J. J. Monaghan (1977) (SPH); V. Springel (2010), *MNRAS* (AREPO); J. M. Stone et al. (2008), *ApJS* (Athena).

#### `SI-42` — Kinetic Plasma Simulation: Particle-in-Cell (PIC) vs. Continuum Vlasov
- **Category:** Stochastic, Kinetic & Astrophysical/Plasma Simulation
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Macroparticle PIC & Hybrid PIC` | `Sparse-Grid & Low-Rank Vlasov` | `Noise-Free Continuum Vlasov–Fokker–Planck`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** For collisionless 6D phase-space plasmas (fusion edge, magnetic reconnection, cosmic shocks), do you accept PIC shot noise or pay the 6D mesh cost of Eulerian Vlasov solvers?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Macroparticle PIC & Hybrid PIC:* Tracking macroparticles through electromagnetic grids conquers the 6D curse of dimensionality effortlessly and runs at blazing speed on GPUs.
  - *Sparse-Grid & Low-Rank Vlasov:* Dynamical low-rank tensor decompositions compress 6D phase space cleanly while avoiding Monte Carlo counting noise.
  - *Noise-Free Continuum Vlasov–Fokker–Planck:* Subtle high-energy tails, Landau damping, and fine phase-space filaments drown in PIC macroparticle shot noise—only continuum Vlasov resolves them cleanly.
- **Primary citations:** C. K. Birdsall & A. B. Langdon (1985), *Plasma Physics via Computer Simulation*; A. Spitkovsky (2008), *ApJL*; E. Sonnendrücker et al. (1999), *J. Comput. Phys.*

#### `SI-43` — Atomistic Evolution Beyond Nanoseconds: Kinetic Monte Carlo vs. Accelerated MD
- **Category:** Stochastic, Kinetic & Astrophysical/Plasma Simulation
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Accelerated / Temperature-Driven MD` | `On-the-Fly Adaptive KMC` | `Cataloged Lattice Kinetic Monte Carlo`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** When simulating crystal growth, catalysis, and radiation damage over seconds to years, how do you bridge the atomic vibrational timescale gap?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Accelerated / Temperature-Driven MD:* Hyperdynamics and parallel-replica dynamics preserve exact continuous atomic trajectories and discover unanticipated collective mechanisms automatically (Voter).
  - *On-the-Fly Adaptive KMC:* Use saddle-point searches (dimer/NEB) dynamically at each visited basin so KMC never relies on a human-guessed event catalog.
  - *Cataloged Lattice Kinetic Monte Carlo:* Precomputed event rates let lattice KMC leap across hours and macroscopic film thicknesses with ease.
- **Primary citations:** A. B. Bortz, M. H. Kalos & J. L. Lebowitz (1975), *J. Comput. Phys.*; A. F. Voter (1997, 2002), *Annu. Rev. Mater. Res.*

#### `SI-44` — High-Dimensional Sampling: Hamiltonian MCMC vs. Sequential & Nested Sampling
- **Category:** Stochastic, Kinetic & Astrophysical/Plasma Simulation
- **Geometry:** `triangle`
- **Poles:** `Gradient-Based Hamiltonian MC (HMC/NUTS)` | `Tempering & Sequential Monte Carlo (SMC)` | `Nested Sampling for Multimodal Evidence`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** When sampling nasty, multimodal, high-dimensional physical distributions or posteriors, which stochastic engine do you trust?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Gradient-Based Hamiltonian MC (HMC/NUTS):* Leveraging gradients via symplectic leapfrog trajectories suppresses random-walk diffusion and scales cleanly to thousands of continuous dimensions.
  - *Tempering & Sequential Monte Carlo (SMC):* Parallel tempering and adaptive particle resampling cross deep energy barriers and parallelize embarrassingly across cluster nodes.
  - *Nested Sampling for Multimodal Evidence:* Compressing nested prior-volume shells directly computes partition functions and Bayesian evidences while handling phase transitions and multiple modes cleanly (Skilling).
- **Primary citations:** S. Duane et al. (1987), *Phys. Lett. B* (HMC); M. D. Hoffman & A. Gelman (2014), *JMLR* (NUTS); J. Skilling (2006), *Bayesian Anal.* (Nested Sampling).

#### `SI-45` — Radiation & Neutrino Transport: Monte Carlo vs. Deterministic Moment Closures
- **Category:** Stochastic, Kinetic & Astrophysical/Plasma Simulation
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Stochastic Monte Carlo Transport` | `Hybrid Implicit MC / Discrete Ordinates` | `Deterministic Moment Closures (M1 / FLD)`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Solving the 7D radiative-transfer equation in supernovae, fusion hohlraums, and reactors is notoriously brutal—where do you compromise?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Stochastic Monte Carlo Transport:* Handles complex 3D geometry, frequency redistribution, and polarization cleanly without artificial beam-crossing artifacts.
  - *Hybrid Implicit MC / Discrete Ordinates:* Discrete ordinates ($S_N$) and implicit Monte Carlo deliver controllable angular resolution without ray effects or severe statistical noise.
  - *Deterministic Moment Closures (M1 / FLD):* Evolving angular moments (energy density and flux) stays smooth, noise-free, and fast enough to couple tightly with 3D MHD and nuclear burning.
- **Primary citations:** J. A. Fleck Jr. & J. D. Cummings (1971), *J. Comput. Phys.* (IMC); C. D. Levermore (1984), *JQSRT* (M1 closure); D. Mihalas & B. W. Mihalas (1984), *Foundations of Radiation Hydrodynamics*.

#### `SI-46` — Free-Energy Calculation Strategy in Molecular Simulation
- **Category:** Stochastic, Kinetic & Astrophysical/Plasma Simulation
- **Geometry:** `triangle`
- **Poles:** `Alchemical Perturbation & Thermodynamic Integration` | `Collective-Variable PMF & Metadynamics` | `Non-Equilibrium Jarzynski / Crooks Pulling`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Computing binding affinities and phase-boundary free energies to within $1\,\text{kcal/mol}$ is a cornerstone of computational chemistry and biophysics—which pathway wins?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Alchemical Perturbation & Thermodynamic Integration:* Mutating ligands cleanly through non-physical thermodynamic cycles yields the gold-standard relative binding free energies used in drug discovery.
  - *Collective-Variable PMF & Metadynamics:* Sampling physical association/dissociation coordinates directly reveals transition barriers and intermediate states that alchemical shortcuts hide.
  - *Non-Equilibrium Jarzynski / Crooks Pulling:* Fast steered trajectories combined via Crooks's bidirectional estimator parallelize massively and avoid slow equilibrium trapping.
- **Primary citations:** R. W. Zwanzig (1954), *J. Chem. Phys.*; D. Frenkel & B. Smit (2002), *Understanding Molecular Simulation*; G. E. Crooks (1999), *Phys. Rev. E*.

### Category 9: Data Assimilation, Digital Twins & Reduced-Order Modeling

#### `SI-47` — Data Assimilation Paradigm: Variational 4D-Var vs. Ensemble Kalman Filters
- **Category:** Data Assimilation, Digital Twins & Reduced-Order Modeling
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Adjoint Variational 4D-Var` | `Hybrid Ensemble-Variational (4DEnVar)` | `Ensemble Kalman & Particle Filters (EnKF)`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** To fuse live noisy sensor observations continuously into nonlinear weather, ocean, or fusion simulations, do you derive exact adjoints or propagate forecast ensembles?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Adjoint Variational 4D-Var:* Exact tangent-linear and adjoint gradients find dynamically consistent trajectories across an entire assimilation window cleanly (ECMWF gold standard).
  - *Hybrid Ensemble-Variational (4DEnVar):* Inject flow-dependent ensemble error covariances directly into variational minimization—uniting both traditions' strengths.
  - *Ensemble Kalman & Particle Filters (EnKF):* Zero painful adjoint code maintenance, native flow-dependent uncertainty estimation, and trivial parallelization across ensemble members (Evensen).
- **Primary citations:** G. Evensen (1994), *J. Geophys. Res.* (EnKF); E. Kalnay (2003), *Atmospheric Modeling, Data Assimilation and Predictability*; K. Law, A. Stuart & K. Zygalakis (2015), *Data Assimilation*.

#### `SI-48` — Physics-Based "Digital Twins": Genuine Paradigm or Buzzword Hype?
- **Category:** Data Assimilation, Digital Twins & Reduced-Order Modeling
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Rebranded Old Simulation Buzzword` | `Valuable When Closed-Loop & UQ-Calibrated` | `Transformative Live Bidirectional Cyber-Physical Paradigm`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Every aerospace, fusion, climate, and medical grant now promises a "digital twin"—does the concept add real scientific substance beyond standard simulation?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Rebranded Old Simulation Buzzword:* Calling an offline FEM mesh a "digital twin" is grant-writing theater unless it updates continuously from live asset telemetry.
  - *Valuable When Closed-Loop & UQ-Calibrated:* A genuine digital twin requires bidirectional sensor assimilation, quantified uncertainty, and verified predictive decision control—otherwise call it a model (Willcox).
  - *Transformative Live Bidirectional Cyber-Physical Paradigm:* Continuously assimilating live telemetry into personalized, real-time reduced-order physics models revolutionizes predictive maintenance, personalized medicine, and Earth forecasting.
- **Primary citations:** National Academies (2023), *Foundational Research Gaps and Future Directions for Digital Twins* (chaired by K. Willcox); M. Grieves (2014).

#### `SI-49` — Reduced-Order Modeling (ROM): Linear POD/Galerkin vs. Nonlinear Neural Manifolds
- **Category:** Data Assimilation, Digital Twins & Reduced-Order Modeling
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Intrusive Projection POD & Reduced Basis` | `Operator Inference & Physics-Preserving ROM` | `Nonlinear Autoencoder Latent Dynamics`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** How should simulators compress million-variable PDE fields into millisecond-fast reduced models for real-time control and design sweeps?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Intrusive Projection POD & Reduced Basis:* Projecting the governing PDE onto proper orthogonal modes preserves stability, conservation structure, and rigorous *a posteriori* error bounds.
  - *Operator Inference & Physics-Preserving ROM:* Non-intrusively learn low-dimensional polynomial operators directly from simulation snapshots while enforcing known energy-preserving structure (Peherstorfer & Willcox).
  - *Nonlinear Autoencoder Latent Dynamics:* Transport-dominated shocks and moving fronts defeat linear Kolmogorov $n$-widths—nonlinear convolutional/graph autoencoders compress them cleanly.
- **Primary citations:** P. Benner, S. Gugercin & K. Willcox (2015), *SIAM Rev.*; B. Peherstorfer & K. Willcox (2016), *Comput. Methods Appl. Mech. Eng.*; K. Lee & K. T. Carlberg (2020), *J. Comput. Phys.*

#### `SI-50` — Multifidelity Uncertainty Quantification & Control Variates
- **Category:** Data Assimilation, Digital Twins & Reduced-Order Modeling
- **Geometry:** `monopole-slider`
- **Poles:** `Exploiting Cheap Low-Fidelity Models to Accelerate High-Fidelity UQ`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** How strongly should computational scientists replace brute-force high-fidelity Monte Carlo with multilevel/multifidelity estimators that pair a few expensive runs with thousands of cheap surrogate runs?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Exploiting Cheap Low-Fidelity Models to Accelerate High-Fidelity UQ:* Multilevel Monte Carlo (Giles) and multifidelity control variates deliver provably unbiased high-fidelity expectations at orders-of-magnitude lower computational cost.
- **Primary citations:** M. B. Giles (2008), *Oper. Res.* (MLMC); B. Peherstorfer, K. Willcox & M. Gunzburger (2018), "Survey of Multifidelity Methods in Uncertainty Propagation, Inference, and Optimization," *SIAM Rev.*

#### `SI-51` — Real-Time Coupling of Experiments & HPC Steering
- **Category:** Data Assimilation, Digital Twins & Reduced-Order Modeling
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Decoupled Offline Post-Run Simulation` | `Fast-Turnaround Between-Shot Guidance` | `Live Closed-Loop Edge-to-HPC Steering`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** Should supercomputers analyze experimental data leisurely offline after beamtime ends, or stream live detector/tokamak/XFEL feeds into real-time HPC loops that steer the next shot?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Decoupled Offline Post-Run Simulation:* Offline analysis prevents hasty real-time miscalibrations and gives physicists quiet time to test competing models carefully.
  - *Fast-Turnaround Between-Shot Guidance:* Running automated reconstruction and forward simulation in the minutes between tokamak discharges or beamline scans maximizes expensive facility productivity.
  - *Live Closed-Loop Edge-to-HPC Steering:* Millisecond neural/ROM surrogates directly steering plasma magnetic fields or synchrotron sample stages unlock dynamic regimes humans can never control manually (Degrave et al.).
- **Primary citations:** J. Degrave et al. (2022), "Magnetic Control of Tokamak Plasmas Through Deep Reinforcement Learning," *Nature*; DOE Integrated Research Infrastructure (IRI) paradigm.

#### `SI-52` — Scientific Data Formats, Provenance & Archival Longevity
- **Category:** Data Assimilation, Digital Twins & Reduced-Order Modeling
- **Geometry:** `trilinear-line`
- **Poles:** `Lightweight Custom Binary / Flat Files` | `Self-Describing Standard Formats (HDF5/Zarr/NetCDF)` | `Full FAIR Provenance & Cloud-Native Analysis-Ready Data`
- **Hovertip (`issue`):** When a single simulation dumps petabytes of checkpoints, how strictly should teams enforce standardized, self-describing, cloud-queryable archival pipelines?
- **Per-pole hovertips:**
  - *Lightweight Custom Binary / Flat Files:* Raw flat binaries maximize raw parallel I/O throughput without fighting bloated library dependencies.
  - *Self-Describing Standard Formats (HDF5/Zarr/NetCDF):* Embedded units, coordinates, and metadata ensure a colleague—or your future self five years later—can actually read the output without guessing endianness.
  - *Full FAIR Provenance & Cloud-Native Analysis-Ready Data:* Chunked cloud-native Zarr archives tagged with code hashes and parameter provenance turn hero simulations into living community observatories (Pangeo/CMIP ethos).
- **Primary citations:** M. D. Wilkinson et al. (2016), *Sci. Data* (FAIR); R. P. Abernathey et al. (2021), *Comput. Sci. Eng.* (Cloud-native Pangeo/Zarr).

## 4. Canonical Archetypes (11 Archetypes)

### 1. The Structure-Preserving Numerical Analyst
- **Summary:** Guards the mathematical soul of simulation—demanding exact discrete conservation, symplectic geometry, rigorous convergence theorems, and clean discretization.
- **Primary Thinkers & Literature:** E. Hairer, C. Lubich & G. Wanner (2006), R. J. LeVeque (2002), L. N. Trefethen (2000), N. J. Higham (2002).
- **Stance Profile:**
  - **Anchors High:** `SI-01` (*Automated Mathematical Deduction* / *Distinct Third Pillar*), `SI-12` (*Conservative Finite Volumes* / *FEM & DG* / *Spectral*), `SI-13` (High *Symplectic & Exact-Conservation Integrators*), `SI-16` (*Strict FP64 Everywhere*), `SI-17` (*Pure First-Principles Solvers Only*), `SI-18` (*Overhyped vs. Classical FEM/FV*), `SI-22` (*Method of Manufactured Solutions*), `SI-28` (*Algorithms Beat Hardware Every Time*), `SI-34` (*Exact Conservation & Clean Convergence*).
  - **Anchors Low:** `SI-16` (*Embrace Low-Precision Tensor Cores*), `SI-17` (*End-to-End Neural Operators*), `SI-20` (*Bitter-Lesson Scale Learns Symmetries Softly*).

### 2. The Exascale HPC & Bare-Metal Engineer
- **Summary:** Pushes hardware to the silicon roofline—fusing cache-aware algorithms, GPU parallelism, and massive scaling to cross physical resolution thresholds.
- **Primary Thinkers & Literature:** J. Dongarra, D. E. Keyes, S. Plimpton (1995), J. L. Gustafson (1988), DOE Exascale co-design tradition.
- **Stance Profile:**
  - **Anchors High:** `SI-08` (*Direct Numerical Simulation (DNS)*), `SI-09` (*Brute-Force Long Unbiased Trajectories*), `SI-11` (*One Ultra-High-Resolution Hero Run*), `SI-14` (*Clean Uniform Structured Grids* or highly scalable AMR), `SI-15` (*Explicit Local Time-Stepping* or *Algorithm–Hardware Co-Design*), `SI-16` (*Mixed Precision with FP64 Accumulation*), `SI-27` (*Fortran & C/C++ Bare Metal*), `SI-28` (*Algorithm–Hardware Co-Design* / *Ride the Exascale GPU Wave*), `SI-29` (*Community Flagship Code* + *Production Software Engineering*), `SI-34` (*Blazing Exascale Throughput*).
  - **Anchors Low:** `SI-02` (*Minimal Models for Insight* alone), `SI-29` (*Rapid Exploratory Prototyping*).

### 3. The Ab Initio Quantum & Atomistic Purist
- **Summary:** Distrusts fitted empirical parameters—insisting on parameter-free quantum electronic structure, lattice field theory, or first-principles dynamics as the only trustworthy foundation.
- **Primary Thinkers & Literature:** W. Kohn, R. Car & M. Parrinello (1985), K. G. Wilson (1974), S. R. White (1992).
- **Stance Profile:**
  - **Anchors High:** `SI-03` (*Reject Ad-Hoc Tuning—Resolve Physics*), `SI-05` (*First-Principles Laws Guarantee Reach*), `SI-06` (*Stochastic Monte Carlo & Lattice* / *Deterministic Particle & MD*), `SI-07` (*Parameter-Free Ab Initio First*), `SI-10` (*Quantum Monte Carlo & Lattice QFT* / *Tensor Networks*), `SI-17` (*Pure First-Principles Solvers Only* or *Hybrid ML-Accelerated Physics* via equivariant MLIPs), `SI-20` (*Hard Equivariance & Exact Conservation*).
  - **Anchors Low:** `SI-03` (*Pragmatic Tuning for System Skill*), `SI-07` (*Top-Down Phenomenological Reach*), `SI-21` (*New Blueprint for Computational Science* if purely black-box).

### 4. The Multiscale & Hierarchical Model Builder
- **Summary:** Navigates the gap between simulation and understanding by linking microphysics to macroscopic emergence through systematic coarse-graining and ladders of idealized models.
- **Primary Thinkers & Literature:** I. M. Held (2005), A. Warshel & M. Levitt (1976), W. E & B. Engquist (2003), S. J. Marrink (2007), W. G. Noid (2013).
- **Stance Profile:**
